*Heading corrected August 14, 2026 — it read "**not yet cut over**" for sixteen days after the cutover. `website-build-cutover-runbook.md` recorded "CUT OVER AND LIVE" on July 29 and this heading was never revised, so the technical source of truth's own table of contents said the site was still hand-written HTML. Verified against the live site, not the repo: `/planes/`, `/en/plans/` and `/pt/planos/` render plan counts computed from `site/_data/plans.js` at build time (164 / 111 / 53), which only an Eleventy build can produce.* ***Standing lesson, fourth time in this project: a status claim inside a heading is the least likely line in the file to be revised and the most likely to be read.***

**Why.** The site was 19 hand-written HTML pages. Measured before starting: 6,583 lines total, 2,411 of them CSS, and **75% of that CSS was duplicate copies** — the ES/EN/PT homepages had byte-identical `<style>` blocks, so every style change was three or more edits. The forcing function wasn't the duplication though, it was **hreflang**: three languages plus a planned blog means every article needs `<link rel="alternate">` tags pointing at its siblings in the other two languages. Hand-maintaining that across 100+ articles is where it stops being feasible. Same concept as a PHP `include`, resolved at build time instead of per request — output is still ordinary static HTML for Caddy.

**Decisions.** Eleventy (v3) over Astro and Hugo: templates are HTML with `{% include %}`, the smallest conceptual surface for AI-assisted editing, and the site has no React anywhere so Astro's islands bought nothing. **Build runs on the VPS**, not GitHub Actions — matches the goal of Hermes eventually doing everything from the box, and keeps a vendor out of the deploy path. The objection to VPS-building (a broken build half-deploying) is handled by building into a temp dir and only rsyncing after a guard confirms a real homepage was produced; see `automation/deploy-website.sh`.

**Structure.** Source in `site/`, output to `_site/` (gitignored, rebuilt at deploy). `site/_data/` holds `site.json` (domain, analytics IDs, price), `nav.json` (menus per language + two members-area variants), `forms.json` (every contact-form string per language). `site/_includes/` holds one base layout plus partials for analytics, nav, footer, the outbound-link tracker, and the contact form. Front-matter flags: `lang`, `transKey`, `navVariant`, `footerVariant`, `pageCss` (string or list), `noindex`, `noClarity`, `noTracking`, `noPrint`. Directory data file `site/members/members.json` applies the members defaults to that whole folder.

**hreflang mechanism.** Pages declare a `transKey`; an Eleventy collection groups every page sharing one and the layout emits the alternate tags plus a language switcher pointing at the *equivalent* page rather than the language homepage. Side effect: the three All-Access pages now have hreflang, which they never had in the hand-written site.

**Verified.** All 19 pages built and diffed against their originals: visible text identical line-for-line on every page, links identical except the intentional deltas below, form fields / sport options / phone-country options identical, and a declaration-level CSS check confirms **zero style rules lost** across all pages.

**Intentional deltas (not regressions):**
- Nav anchors are now absolute (`/#coaching`, not `#coaching`) so one nav definition is correct on every page of a language — the hand-written site needed a homepage variant and a subpage variant. Behaviour from the homepage is unchanged (still an in-page jump).
- The logo is a link on all pages; it was a plain `<div>` only on the three homepages. `text-decoration: none` was duplicated into every other page's CSS for exactly this reason and now lives once in `site.css`.
- Language switcher on the All-Access pages now goes to the equivalent All-Access page instead of dumping the visitor on the language homepage.

**Bugs caught by the diff, worth recording because none were visible by reading the code:**
1. Generating the homepage's plan card-links from the nav data changed their anchor text — the nav uses short Title Case labels, those card links use longer sentence-case anchor text chosen for SEO. Same destinations, deliberately different copy. Over-DRY mistake, reverted with a comment.
2. `site/README.md` was being built and published as a live page at `/README/`. Fixed with `.eleventyignore`.
3. Multi-stylesheet front matter serialised as a quoted string instead of a YAML list, leaving five members pages with **no CSS at all** — the built HTML looked fine, only the rendered stylesheet list showed it.
4. The testing-protocols page's `<footer class="no-print">` lost its class, which would have printed the footer on a page explicitly designed to be printed.

**Analytics unified across the whole site (decided July 26, 2026).** In the hand-written site, Microsoft Clarity and the GA4 outbound-link conversion tracker were on the public pages and on **none** of the 9 members pages — copy-paste drift, not a decision. Iván's call: every page now gets the same stack as the Spanish homepage — GA4, Clarity, and the conversion tracker — defined once in `analytics.njk` and `tracking.njk` rather than pasted per page. This does mean Clarity records session replays of logged-in subscribers; members pages remain `noindex` so they stay publicly undiscoverable. Per-page opt-out is still available via `noClarity: true` / `noTracking: true` in front matter. Verified after the change: all 19 pages carry all three tags. Also retired in the same pass: `website/googleTag.html`, a scratch file holding GA4 setup notes that was being publicly served at `/googleTag.html` for no reason — its content is now a comment in `site/_data/site.json`.

**Inconsistency found and deliberately preserved rather than silently changed:**
- The members area grew three different nav shapes and three different footers. Both real nav variants are now defined in `nav.json`; `kettlebell` keeps its own inline nav because it is an outlier in every sense — English copy on a Spanish members area, its own design language, and 173 of its 204 CSS lines unique to it. Worth unifying, flagged not fixed.

**Three deploy-path failures, July 26–27, 2026 — all fixed, one shared root cause: the VPS held state the repo didn't control** (an rsync'd `0700` webroot permission causing a site-wide 403, a local commit diverging the branch and silently halting deploys, and a stale copy of the deploy script that couldn't pull its own fix). The invariant the fixes converge on: **the VPS owns nothing** — it resets to origin (`fetch` + `reset --hard` + `clean -fd`), rebuilds into a temp directory, re-copies its own script after a proven run, and generated data lives outside the checkout (e.g. `PLAN_LINK_STATUS` → `~/.hermes/plan_link_status.json`). A fourth bug in the same pass: the post-deploy verification itself false-alarmed (curl sends no SNI for a bare IP; fixed with `--resolve`). Full narratives, diagnosis commands (`namei -l`), and lessons: `build-log.md`; operational rules: `website-build-cutover-runbook.md` ("The VPS checkout is disposable").

~~**Not cut over yet.** `website/` is untouched and still in the repo; the build now produces the served HTML but the old hand-written folder has not been deleted.~~ ***Struck August 14, 2026, on Iván's instruction — "plans are not hand-written HTML, fix that."*** *The cutover happened **July 29, 2026** and Phase 5 cleanup completed the same day: `images/` and `guias/` moved into `site/assets/`, the superseded `website/` subfolders were deleted, and `website/` now holds **only** the permanent `hubfs` route that TrainingPeaks hotlinks across ~300 live plans — that is not migration debt. **Nothing on this site is hand-written HTML today**, least of all the plan pages: all 328 are generated by three Eleventy pagination shells from `site/_data/plans.js`. The paragraph below it ("Cutover sequence: install Node on the VPS → …") is a completed checklist, not a to-do list. Left readable as the record of what the cutover involved.* The cutover sequence, for the record: install Node on the VPS → replace `~/.hermes/deploy-website.sh` with the version in `automation/` → run it manually once and diff `/var/www/triaperformance` against the current site → then delete `website/` from the repo and move `images/`, `hubfs/`, `guias/` into `site/assets/` (the Eleventy config currently passthrough-copies them from their old location).

**Sitemap + robots.txt added, July 28, 2026 — GSC "Duplicate without user-selected canonical."** After cutover, Google Search Console flagged this reason for the first time. Investigation ruled out a template bug: `base.njk`'s canonical tag (`{{ page.url | absoluteUrl }}`) is unconditional and self-referencing, verified correct and distinct on every built page (checked `index.html`, `en/index.html`, `pt/index.html`, `all-access/index.html`, `en/all-access/index.html` directly in `_site/`); no www/non-www mismatch (`absoluteUrl`'s base is a single hardcoded string); no stale orphaned pages (`deploy-website.sh` uses `rsync -a --delete`). What was actually missing: **no `sitemap.xml` or `robots.txt` existed anywhere in the repo** — the migration shipped hreflang but nothing telling Google which of the resulting URLs are canonical at a site level, which is a known contributing factor for this exact GSC category right after a structural change on a multi-language site.

Fix: `site/sitemap.njk` (permalink `/sitemap.xml`, `eleventyExcludeFromCollections: true` so it doesn't try to list itself) walks `collections.all`, skips anything with `noindex: true` (i.e. all of `/members/*`, via the existing `members.json` directory default — nothing new to maintain there), and for each surviving page emits `<loc>`, `<lastmod>` (from Eleventy's own file date), and the same hreflang alternates as the HTML `<head>` (via `xhtml:link`, pulling from `collections.translations[transKey]` and `site.langOrder` — identical mechanism to `base.njk`, so the two can't drift). `site/robots.txt` is a static file passed through via a new `addPassthroughCopy` entry in `.eleventy.js`, disallowing `/members/` (belt-and-braces; that path is already `noindex` and gated by Caddy `forward_auth`) and pointing at the sitemap.

Verified with a real build (`npx @11ty/eleventy --output=/tmp/tp-sitemap-test`): output is 16 URLs — the 3 homepages, 3 All-Access pages, 4 plan pages, 3 blog indexes, 3 blog articles, in all three languages — zero members pages, hreflang blocks match the HTML exactly, XML parses clean. Next step — tracked in `open-loops.md` (the indexing-check item), not here — this doc keeps no open-item list.

Sitemap submitted July 28, 2026 and confirmed live via `curl https://triaperformance.com/sitemap.xml` and `curl https://triaperformance.com/robots.txt` — both serving correctly from the real domain, matching the local build output exactly.

**Full analytics/SEO-tooling sanity check across the migration, July 28, 2026.** Prompted by the cutover, checked every tool listed in `infrastructure.html` §9 survived Eleventy intact, verified against a real build's actual HTML output rather than just reading the templates:
- **GA4** (`G-T69KEHW59J`) and **Microsoft Clarity** (`xq3g7pvr9i`) — both present on all 25 built pages (grepped `gtag/js?id=` and `clarity.ms/tag` across every file in `_site/`, zero pages missing either). They live once in `site/_includes/partials/analytics.njk`, included unconditionally by `base.njk`, so this can't drift per-page the way the old hand-written site did (Clarity was missing from all 9 members pages there — see the July 26 entry above).
- **GA4 conversion-event tracker** (`begin_checkout`, `select_plan`, `whatsapp_click`, in `tracking.njk`) — also present on all 25 pages; only conditional on `noTracking`, which no page currently sets.
- **Google Search Console** — a Domain property verified via a DNS TXT record (not a per-page meta tag or script), so it was never at risk from the template migration at all. Confirmed unaffected.
- **Bing Webmaster Tools** and **Ahrefs Webmaster Tools** — both were added via "Import from Google Search Console" (see `infrastructure.html` §9), which piggybacks on the same DNS verification rather than adding their own site-verification tag. Same conclusion: nothing in these tools' setup depends on page HTML, so the Eleventy migration could not have broken them. No verification action needed on these two beyond confirming GSC itself is still verified (it is — same DNS record, untouched by the site rebuild).

Net finding: **nothing in the analytics/SEO tool stack was actually at risk from the migration** — GA4/Clarity/conversion-tracking are template-level and were spot-checked in real build output above; GSC/Bing/Ahrefs are DNS-level and structurally can't be touched by a template change. The one real, concrete gap the migration introduced was the missing sitemap.xml/robots.txt (fixed above), not a regression in any of the previously-installed tools.

A standing "new/modified page" checklist covering all of this (analytics inheritance, `noindex`, `transKey`, sitemap inclusion, why GSC/Bing/Ahrefs need no per-page action) is now in `triaperformance-project-instructions.md` under "Technical/build work," so this doesn't need re-verifying by hand on every future page.

## 16. Eleventy Phase 5 cleanup — completed, scope revised (July 29, 2026)

Original plan (cutover runbook steps 17–19) was to move `images`, `hubfs`, and `guias` into `site/assets/` and delete all of `website/`. Revised mid-session: `website/hubfs` is now **permanent**, not migration debt. TrainingPeaks' own marketplace pages hotlink the exact route `triaperformance.com/hubfs/tp_marketplace/*.png` across roughly 300 live plan listings — confirmed directly with Iván. There's no way to update ~300 external references after the fact, so unlike everything else in `website/`, this one folder (and its `.eleventy.js` passthrough line) stays exactly where it is, indefinitely.

Only `images` and `guias` were moved (`git mv` into `site/assets/`). Everything that referenced the old `/images/...` and `/guias/...` paths was updated to `/assets/...`: `site/assets/css/site.css` (hero background), the 3 marathon-plan blog articles, and `site/members/index.njk`.

Two real bugs found and fixed along the way, neither caused by the migration itself, both just never noticed until this session touched the links:
- **`/guias/` had no index page and always 404'd**, even before the move — the two links pointing at a bare `/guias/` directory were relying on directory listing that was never actually enabled anywhere. Built a real page instead of patching around it: `site/members/guias/index.njk`, gated behind the existing `/members/*` auth (Iván's call — the guides are a subscriber benefit, not a public lead magnet, despite the FAQ copy saying "no other login needed," which is still accurate since it just means no *separate* login beyond the one members-area session). First draft of this page used `members-home.css` for its card-grid styling, which produced a dark-content/white-nav mismatch — `members-home.css` is a one-off dark theme scoped to the members *homepage's* own nav class (`.site-nav`) and was never meant to pair with the regular subpage nav class (`.site-nav-sticky`), which every other `/members/*` subpage uses and is light-themed. Fixed by dropping `members-home.css` and just using `members.css` + the base `site.css` card/label classes, matching every other subpage.
- **The old `/guias/*.pdf` URLs are linked from already-sent live email sequences**, so they can't just be updated. Added 3 explicit Caddy redirects (`/guias/zonas-de-entrenamiento.pdf`, `/guias/pre-entreno.pdf`, `/guias/intervalos.pdf`, each → its `/assets/guias/...` equivalent, `permanent`) — deliberately not a `/guias/*` wildcard, since that would also catch requests for the new real `/members/guias/` page and bounce visitors into the (index-less) `/assets/guias/` folder instead.

Also found, logged as a separate content item rather than fixed here: the 3 guides only exist in Spanish. The EN/PT marathon blog articles used to link to the Spanish PDF anyway — removed that paragraph from both articles as a stopgap (Iván's call) rather than leave a mismatched-language link; real translations would be new content work, not an infra fix.

One process note for future sessions: a batch of edits (CSS fix, template link fixes, the new guides page) sat as uncommitted working-tree changes after a `git rm`+`commit`+`push` that only picked up what was already staged — worth remembering that `git commit` without `-A` or a prior `git add` silently leaves unstaged modifications behind, which showed up live as a broken hero image and a stale link until caught and pushed in a second commit.

*Update, July 29, 2026 — GSC "Duplicate without user-selected canonical" real root cause found and fixed.* The one flagged URL (`https://triaperformance.com/`) had Google selecting `https://www.triaperformance.com/` as its canonical instead of the declared tag, despite the template being correct (per above). Actual cause: the live Caddyfile grouped both hostnames into one site block (`triaperformance.com, www.triaperformance.com { ... }`), serving identical content as two separate live 200 pages with no redirect between them — a server-level issue, not a template one, so the earlier "no www/non-www mismatch" check (which only looked at `absoluteUrl`'s hardcoded base in the template) didn't catch it. Fixed by splitting into two Caddy blocks: the apex domain keeps all existing directives (members-area auth gate, contact-form proxy, headers) unchanged, and `www.triaperformance.com` is now its own block doing `redir https://triaperformance.com{uri} permanent`. Verified live: `curl -I https://www.triaperformance.com/` → `301` to the apex domain, `curl -I https://triaperformance.com/` → normal `200`. The other flagged categories in GSC that session were all non-issues: `/es/` 404 (expected — Spanish now serves at root, no `/es/` path in the current site), the `http://www.triaperformance.com/` "page with redirect" entry (just Caddy's automatic HTTP→HTTPS upgrade, unrelated), and three `?hsLang=...` URLs last crawled in February (pre-dates the July cutover entirely, stale HubSpot-era relics, self-resolving).

*Update, August 30, 2026 — the `/es/` 404 is no longer expected, it is redirected. **Deployed and confirmed live by Iván the same day.*** A third-party marketplace listing (not editable by Iván, edit request pending and slow) points at `https://triaperformance.com/es/`. `automation/Caddyfile` now carries `@esPrefix path_regexp esprefix ^/es(?:/(.*))?$` → `redir @esPrefix /{re.esprefix.1} permanent`, so `/es/` lands on `/` and `/es/<anything>` lands on `/<anything>`. Kept permanently rather than as a stopgap: these URLs never existed on this site, so there is nothing they can shadow. **Deliberately not `path /es/*`** — that matcher cannot preserve the rest of the path, so every `/es/...` URL would land on the homepage. **And deliberately non-capturing (`(?:/(.*))?`)** — the obvious `^/es/?(.*)$` also matches `/espanol` and `/estiramientos` and would redirect them to `/panol` and `/tiramientos`.

## 17. Plan Storefront Phase 1, part 1 — catalog + individual plan-page template (built July 30, 2026; **live, confirmed August 6, 2026**)

First real build session on the storefront's core deliverable (`open-loops.md`'s one big branch, `growth-roadmap.md` storefront section). Scope this session: the individual plan-page template and the 3 catalog pages with facet filters. Not in scope: the AI plan picker (Phase 2), B-lite (Phase 3), direct checkout (deferred behind a revenue trigger). Everything below was built and locally verified (`npx eleventy --output=...`, clean build, zero template-syntax leaks, page counts confirmed against the data).

*Update, August 6, 2026 (fourth pass — intent hub pages and site-wide nav fix). The storefront went from 4 category pages to **18 plan pages**, and the nav was pointing at the wrong places in all three languages.*

*__Nav.__ ES "Planes" linked to `/planes/running/` — a filtered view — rather than the catalog. Worse, EN "Plans" and PT "Planos" pointed at `#planes`, an anchor on their own homepage, so **neither language had any nav route to its catalog at all** since the storefront shipped. All three now land on the unfiltered catalog. Dropdowns: ES 8 entries, EN 6 (its first), PT 4 (its first).*

*__New hubs, built on the existing catalog component with a preset__ (`presetSport` / `presetDistance` / `presetFeature`), not as new page types: ES ciclismo 33, natación 19, ironman 13 · EN running 45, cycling 24, swimming 17, ironman 5, weight-loss 34 · PT maratona 14, ciclismo 3, ironman 2. Selection was inventory-led: cycling (60 across languages) and swimming (40) were the two largest categories with no page and no nav entry anywhere.*

*__`presetDistances` added to the catalog component__ — a list rather than a single value, because the Ironman hub spans `Half` and `Full` and neither distance has the inventory for its own page (7 and 6 in Spanish). OR-within-a-group gives the union for free. One catalog per page remains a hard constraint: the mobile chip JS keys panels by an owner id restarting at `g0` per container and looks the scrim up document-wide, so two grids on one page would collide. Noted in the component header.*

*__Closing coaching CTA__ (`partials/coaching-cta.njk`, copy in `planUi.json` ×3) — the three catalog index pages ended on the bare grid with no next step, while every other plan page promoted 1:1 coaching.*

*__Shared `planes-hub.css`__ rather than a fifth through eleventh copy of the near-identical per-category stylesheets. The four legacy files (`planes-running`, `-triatlon`, `-hyrox`, `-bajar-de-peso`) are ~95% duplicates of each other and were deliberately left alone — folding them in is a separate change with its own regression surface.*

*Verified in real Chromium across all 18 pages: exact plan counts, single `<h1>`, CTA present, hreflang pairing, nav dropdown contents, and the mobile chip bar on the new hubs. Nothing regressed.*

*Update, August 6, 2026 (third finding, plan-page content) — **every plan page now carries generated content sections**: "Who this plan is for", "How the training works", "What you need", and "How it works in TrainingPeaks", with the All-Access module moved to sit after them and before the closing buy CTA. Written from the plan's own data (inventory flags + the weekly-breakdown crawl), not copied from the TrainingPeaks listings — the hand-written listing rewrites cover **20 of 321 plans (6%)**, so copying them would have filled 20 pages and left 301 as they were, and reusing that text verbatim would have set these pages against `trainingpeaks.com` for the same queries on a domain that outranks this one. Two derived fields added in `site/_data/plans.js`: `weeklyTotals` (sessions/week, average weekly hours, longest session — summing only duration-based activities, so a swim plan measured in metres reports sessions and no hours rather than a fabricated number) and `siblings` (nearest easier/harder/shorter/longer plan in the same sport and distance, for the "not the right fit?" cross-links — 296 of 321 pages have at least one). Sibling refs are **flattened to `{pageUrl, difficulty, weeks}` deliberately**: storing the plan object makes A→B→A cycles that blow Eleventy's data-cascade merge with "Maximum call stack size exceeded" before a page renders. Copy lives in `planUi.json`, 28 keys × ES/EN/PT. Product schema description now carries the measured sessions/hours instead of restating the title. Verified in real Chromium across ES/EN/PT and three data shapes (power+strength, swim-in-metres, HR marathon): 4 blocks and 15–16 bullets per page, no unresolved placeholders, single column on mobile.*

*Update, August 6, 2026 (same day, second finding) — **the facet filters were live but non-functional, now fixed.** `catalog-filters.js` was correct throughout; `plan-page.css` set `display: flex`/`grid` on `.catalog-card`, `.catalog-grid` and `.facet-options label`, and an author `display` rule silently outranks the UA stylesheet's `[hidden] { display: none }`. So the script hid the right cards and the browser drew them anyway. Fixed with three `[hidden]` author rules (specificity 0,2,0 — no `!important`). Blast radius was wider than the reported symptom: the four category pages preset a facet and rely on this JS to narrow on load, so `/planes/running/`, `/triatlon/`, `/hyrox/` and `/bajar-de-peso/` were each serving all 179 ES plans (HYROX showed 179 instead of 7). Full write-up in `build-log.md`. **Standing lesson: never hide a styled element with the `hidden` attribute alone** — ship an author-level `[hidden]` rule or toggle a class, because `element.hidden === true` while the element is still painted is a failure no DOM check will catch.*

*Update, August 6, 2026 — **live.** This section, and `open-loops.md`, both said "not deployed" for a week after the fact; the July 30 commits went out on the normal cron pull and the storefront has been serving since. Verified against the live site rather than inferred from the repo: `/planes/` 179 plans, `/en/plans/` 108, `/pt/planos/` 34 — 321 total, an exact match to `is_published=TRUE` in the inventory CSV. Facets, weekly-breakdown table, All-Access module, inline email capture and UTM + `plan_id` redirects all rendering; `Product`/`Offer`/`Brand` schema present in the built output, no `aggregateRating` as designed. The email-capture **backend** remains the genuinely open piece (see below) — the front end posting to a route whose n8n side may not be Active is a silent failure, not a visible one, which is exactly why it survived unnoticed. Standing lesson, third time in this project: deploy state is cheap to check against a live URL and expensive to carry forward as an assumption.*

**Data layer** — `site/_data/plans.js` extended (not duplicated; the file already existed from the content-engine Phase 0 work) to join `data/plan_weekly_breakdown.csv` (765 rows / 301 plans) onto the existing inventory load, and to compute per-plan: `displayName` (emoji stripped for display — 251/321 published plan names carry one), `slug` (`slugify(name) + "-" + plan_id`, guarantees uniqueness even where names collide across difficulty tiers), `pageUrl` (this site's own plan-page URL, not the TP link), `weeksBucket` (facet bucket: 1-8/9-12/13-18/19+), and `byLanguage` groups (es 179 / en 108 / pt 34 of 321 total linkable plans). 20 published plans have no weekly-breakdown row; `weeklyBreakdown: null` and the template renders a fallback sentence instead of an empty table — verified in the build (found and checked one: `maraton-tokyo-2026-...-439350`).

**URL scheme** (confirmed with Iván before generating): individual plans at `/planes/p/<slugified-name>-<plan_id>/` (ES), `/en/plans/p/.../` (EN), `/pt/planos/p/.../` (PT) — name-first for SEO, ID-suffix for uniqueness. Catalogs at `/planes/`, `/en/plans/`, `/pt/planos/`. No `transKey` on individual plan pages (Iván confirmed: plans in different languages are not translations of each other, no forced hreflang between them); the 3 catalog index pages do share a `transKey` (`plan-catalog`).

**Individual plan template** — one shared partial (`site/_includes/partials/plan-page-content.njk`) included by 3 thin Eleventy pagination shells (`site/planes/p/plan.njk`, `site/en/plans/p/plan.njk`, `site/pt/planos/p/plan.njk`), each paginating `plans.byLanguage.<lang>` with `size: 1`. Renders: name/price/duration/difficulty/sport/distance, the weekly-breakdown table (sessions/week, avg duration, longest session, rest days — degrades gracefully when absent), feature flags (strength/weight-loss/time-goal), the All-Access promo module (`site/_includes/partials/all-access-module.njk`, price+URL from the new `site/_data/allAccess.json` — $39.99 ES/EN, $29.99 PT, matching the live All-Access pages), inline skippable email capture next to the buy button, the "Buy on TrainingPeaks" CTA (UTM + `plan_id` appended via a new `withUtm` Eleventy filter, built on the URL API so it's correct regardless of existing query strings), and Product schema (JSON-LD, price + availability — deliberately no `aggregateRating`, since review-per-plan sourcing is still an open question per the brief, and fabricating ratings would be a Rich-Results-Test violation, not a shortcut). Iván still needs to run the actual Rich Results Test against a live URL post-deploy — flagged in the roadmap for exactly this moment, not done here (no live URL exists yet).

**Catalog + facets** — `site/_includes/partials/plan-catalog-grid.njk`, included by the 3 new catalog index pages and (see below) the 4 existing category pages. Static site, no backend: every plan card renders server-side from the data (crawlable and functional with JS off) with `data-*` attributes (sport/distance/difficulty/weeksbucket/features); `site/assets/js/catalog-filters.js` only toggles visibility client-side (OR within a facet group, AND across groups — standard e-commerce pattern). Facet option lists (sport/distance/difficulty) are computed from the actual data via a new `facetValues` Eleventy filter, not hand-maintained, so they can't drift from the CSV.

**The 4 existing hand-written category pages** (`site/planes/{triatlon,running,hyrox,bajar-de-peso}/index.njk`) had their hardcoded plan-card grids (hand-pasted TP links, no UTM/plan_id, no facets) replaced with the same catalog component, preset to that category's filter — same URLs preserved (they're indexed), same intro/hero copy preserved verbatim, only the grid section changed. Presets needed 3 different mechanisms because the source data models these three categories differently: `presetSport="Triathlon"` / `presetSport="Running"` (sport is a real CSV column), `presetDistance="HYROX"` (HYROX is a `distance` value under `sport=Running`, not its own sport, in the source data — this was not obvious going in), `presetFeature="weightloss"` (weight-loss is the `weight_loss` boolean flag, not a sport or distance). Getting this right required checking the actual CSV rather than assuming the category page's implicit taxonomy matched the data model.

**Two real Eleventy bugs found and fixed while building this, worth remembering for future pagination work:**
- Front-matter string values (`title`, `description`) are **not** auto-templated the way `permalink` is — a first pass shipped `<title>{{ plan.displayName }} — ...</title>` as literal text in the rendered HTML. Fix: move those two keys into an `eleventyComputed` block, which *is* rendered through the configured `dataTemplateEngine` (njk, per `.eleventy.js`) with the pagination item's own data in scope.
- That fix then double-escaped any plan name containing `&` (`&amp;amp;`) — the `eleventyComputed` string autoescapes once, then `base.njk`'s own `{{ title }}` autoescapes again. Fixed with `| safe` inside the `eleventyComputed` template (the same pattern the existing contact-form.njk already used for `| dump | safe`, just not one I'd connected to this failure mode until it showed up).
- Pagination's `addAllPagesToCollections` defaults to `false` in Eleventy 3.x — only the *first* item of a paginated template lands in `collections.all` unless you opt in. First build silently produced a sitemap with 3 plan pages instead of 321 (one per language's pagination "page 1"). Fixed by adding `pagination.addAllPagesToCollections: true` to all 3 plan-page shells. Sitemap now correctly carries all 321 individual plan URLs + the 3 new catalog URLs (340 total, up from the pre-storefront baseline — in line with the "~330 URLs, expected, fine" note in the growth roadmap).

~~**Email capture — spec'd, not live.**~~ **Email capture — LIVE.** *(Resolved August 14, 2026: **Iván confirms the plan-lead workflow is Active in n8n and the capture is live on every plan page**, and that `PLAN_CATALOG` exists in Twenty's `leadSource` enum. This line said "not live" while the closing note at the end of this same §17 said "email-capture backend live and tested end-to-end Aug 6" — **the section contradicted itself for eight days**, and two other files (`plan-lead-pipeline-runbook.md`, `site/assets/js/plan-capture.js`) sided with the wrong half. All three corrected in this same pass. The closing note was right; this line was the copy that never got updated when the backend went live.)* ~~Front end ships today (every plan page has the capture form, posts to `/api/plan-lead`); nothing on the backend exists yet.~~ Full spec, Caddy route, and a ready-to-import n8n workflow (`automation/plan-lead-workflow.json`, cloned from `contact-form-workflow.json` with the phone-branch removed) are in the new `plan-lead-pipeline-runbook.md`. ~~Blocked on: Iván adding `PLAN_CATALOG` to Twenty's `leadSource` enum (Settings → Data Model) and confirming it with a real API call~~ — **unblocked; `PLAN_CATALOG` confirmed present in the enum by Iván, Aug 14, 2026.** *(The caution below was right and is kept:)* not assumed, per standing practice, since Twenty introspection is disabled on this instance and every past enum assumption in this project has needed a live check to confirm. One live design decision flagged in the runbook rather than assumed: whether plan-catalog leads should enter the same WhatsApp coaching-nurture watchdog as contact-form leads — defaulted to `excludeFromSequence: true` (colder signal, didn't ask to be contacted) but easy to flip.

~~**What's still open for the next slice:**~~ — **all resolved.** Deployed (it had already been serving on the cron pull when this was written); Rich Results Test run Aug 6 and green; email-capture backend live and tested end-to-end Aug 6; race landing pages unblocked and now the next big branch. Only the `aggregateRating` review-to-plan mapping remains, and that is a strategy question, not a build one — see `growth-roadmap.md` §Training Plan Storefront, Open questions.

*Update, August 8, 2026 — **every plan count in this section is a historical figure and should be read as one.*** When §17 was written the catalog was **321 published (179 ES / 108 EN / 34 PT)**, and the derived numbers here — 251/321 names carrying emoji, 296/321 pages with a sibling link, 20 plans with no weekly-breakdown row, 340 sitemap URLs — are all against that denominator. They were correct on the day and are left exactly as written, per this document's append-don't-rewrite rule.

***Corrected August 17, 2026 — the paragraph below is stale and was read aloud by Hermes as current. The count is 328 published (ES 164 / EN 111 / PT 53), owned by `data/training_plans_inventory.csv`, every row of which is published. Read everything below as a historical record ending August 10, 2026.*** *How this was found: on the first Telegram message after the model migration, Hermes was asked the published plan count. **It read the CSV correctly — it reported 328 rows, ES 164 / EN 111 / PT 53 — and then led with 303 from this paragraph**, labelling the stale figure "the reconciled live catalog count" and the CSV "the raw inventory file count." It inverted the source of truth, and it was right to: **this file is the technical source of truth and this sentence states a count in the present tense**, while the docs that carry the correct number carry it as `~~303~~ **328**`. Between a confident present-tense claim and a struck-through one, the confident one wins. ***The append-don't-rewrite rule protects the reasoning, not the tense** — a superseded figure has to be marked superseded at the point it is stated, or it goes on asserting itself.*)

The live count ~~is now~~ **was, as of August 10, 2026,** **301 published (ES 164 / EN 108 / PT 29)**: 17 race-stamped plans retired Aug 6, plus one the sweep missed (`612978`, Maratón de Río de Janeiro 2026) caught and unpublished Aug 8. *Update, August 10, 2026 — **303 (ES 164 / EN 110 / PT 29)**. `442088` and `439815`, the EN full-distance Ironman Build and Peak plans, were live on TrainingPeaks all along and carried stale `is_published=FALSE` flags; confirmed by Iván and corrected. ## 18. CoachMatch pipeline reliability fixes + full VPS script/config migration into the repo (July 31, 2026)

Three real, independent bugs found and fixed in the same session, all via the same discipline: read the actual node input/output or execution data before proposing a fix, rather than guessing from the symptom. Also closed out the "blind scripts" problem — every Python script and the live Caddy config that used to exist only on the VPS now lives in the repo.

**Bug 1 — WhatsApp follow-up watchdog never advanced past the first nudge.** `twenty_followup_check.py` (the daily Hermes cron, §8) was read-only by original design — it built a `wa.me` deep link and posted it to Telegram but never wrote back to Twenty. Consequence: `whatsappTouchCount` stayed at 1 forever and the same generic nudge text repeated daily, so a real third/closing message never fired. Rebuilt so the script now: picks the message by `whatsappTouchCount` (1 → the existing check-in nudge, now "message 2"; 2 → a new closing message with Instagram/reviews/website links, "message 3"), writes `whatsappTouchCount` and `lastTouchpoint` back to Twenty the moment it builds that day's nudge (optimistic — the nudge itself counts as the touch, matching how Iván was doing this manually), excludes leads with no phone on file entirely (nothing to click, was cluttering the digest), and auto-sets `leadStatus = LOST_NO_RESPONSE` once a lead has been silent 7+ days after message 3 (only `leadStatus` changes at that point — `lastTouchpoint` is left alone since no new touch happened). Now archived at `automation/twenty_followup_check.py`. **Not yet run against a real batch as of this writing** — first live run is Aug 1.

**Bug 2 — email nurture (`emailTouchCount`) stuck at 1, a GraphQL type mismatch.** The "CoachMatch – Email Nurture 2-3" n8n workflow (Schedule Trigger, daily, emails 2/3 — see §8) declared its query variable as `$maxTouch: Float!` but compared it against `emailTouchCount`, an Int-typed field in Twenty's schema (introspection is disabled on this instance, so this was never confirmed at build time — guessed, and wrong). The type mismatch failed the entire query silently every day, before any candidate was ever fetched, on an unattended cron nobody was watching. Fixed by dropping the variable and hardcoding `emailTouchCount: { lt: 3 }` directly in the query string (same pattern already used in the WhatsApp script — no variable, nothing to mismatch). Confirmed live: manually executed, a real email went out, and the follow-up mutation returned `data.updatePerson.id` matching the correct person with no `errors`. Archived at `automation/coachmatch-email-nurture-2-3.json`.

**Bug 3 — leads stuck at `leadStatus: NEW` with blank touch counts despite an email actually going out.** Root cause, found by inspecting a real stuck lead (a Chilean athlete) whose execution turned out to contain **two** leads at once (n8n's IMAP trigger can deliver more than one new CoachMatch email per poll if they arrive close together — confirmed live, ~1 in 5 executions). Four Code nodes in `coachmatch-lead-automation.json` — **Code in JavaScript3** (duplicate-lead Telegram message), **4** (WhatsApp outreach summary), **5** (WhatsApp follow-up mutation), **6** (email follow-up mutation) — were written single-item style (`$('NodeName').item...`, `return [{ json: {...} }]`) but never had their execution mode explicitly set, so they defaulted to n8n's "Run Once for All Items." With 2 leads in one execution, each of these nodes ran exactly **once total** instead of once per lead, silently updating/notifying only the first lead in the batch (the built-in Send Email node handles multiple items correctly natively, which is why the email itself always went out to everyone — only the custom follow-up mutations were affected). Fixed by explicitly setting `"mode": "runOnceForEachItem"` on all four nodes and changing their `return` from a one-element array to a single object (required in that mode). Archived, live-fixed and reported working (Iván: "Working now. Good bug catch").

**Manual cleanup required from bugs 2 and 3, one-time.** Leads processed by the buggy code before these fixes need their Twenty fields corrected by hand (both an incorrect `emailTouchCount`/`whatsappTouchCount`/`leadStatus`, or a missing one) to avoid a duplicate send next cycle — done ad hoc as each was found (e.g. the Chilean lead, Agustín Calderón) rather than as a bulk pass; no comprehensive backfill query was run to catch every historical instance of either bug. Worth a one-time audit if stale-looking `NEW`/blank-count leads keep turning up.

**Full script + config migration — the "blind scripts" problem, closed.** Before this session, three Python scripts and the live Caddy config existed *only* on the VPS, edited by hand over SSH/nano when they needed to change — no version history, no review, easy to silently drift from anything documented here. All four are now tracked in the repo:
- `automation/twenty_followup_check.py`, `automation/update_lead_status.py` — both invoked by Hermes's own job/agent mechanisms via a fixed filename (`~/.hermes/scripts/<name>.py`). Rather than change how Hermes calls them, the physical file at that fixed path was replaced with a ~6-line dispatcher (`git pull` on the repo mirror, then `runpy.run_path()` the real script from `automation/`) — Hermes's config never needs to change again, only the repo file does.
- `automation/analytics/sync_pixel_data.py` — invoked via a plain crontab line (`~/.analytics/venv/bin/python ...`), no dispatcher needed since crontab is freely user-editable text; the line itself now does `cd ~/.hermes/triaperformance-docs && git pull -q &&` before invoking the repo copy.
- `automation/Caddyfile` — the live `/etc/caddy/Caddyfile` config. Folded into the existing daily `deploy-website.sh` cron job rather than given its own mechanism: every run now diffs the repo's copy against the live one, and only on a difference does it validate the *repo's* copy with `caddy validate` before ever touching the live file — a bad commit fails loudly there and leaves the running config alone — then `cp`s it over and `systemctl reload caddy`. Confirmed the executing user already has passwordless `sudo` for both commands (`sudo -n caddy validate ...` returned a clean "Valid configuration", exit 0 — no sudoers change was needed).

**New standing practice, going forward (also added to the project's custom instructions):** every Python script that runs on the VPS — via Hermes cron, Hermes's agent, or plain crontab — gets written into `automation/` in this repo from day one, never authored directly on the box. The same now applies to Caddy config changes. The only manual, non-git step left anywhere in this pipeline is the n8n workflow UI itself (still no live API/MCP connection to n8n), and even there, every workflow gets archived back to the repo the same session it's built or changed.

**Also codified from bug 3, specifically for n8n Code nodes going forward:** always set the node's execution mode explicitly (`Run Once for All Items` vs `Run Once for Each Item`) rather than leaving it on the default — code written in single-item style (`.item`, `.first()`, a hardcoded one-element `return`) needs "Run Once for Each Item" or it will silently misbehave the moment more than one item reaches it in a single execution, exactly as happened here. A single-item manual test in the n8n UI cannot catch this class of bug — it needs a real or replayed multi-item execution.

*Update, August 1, 2026 — the §18 dispatcher for `twenty_followup_check.py` broke on its actual first live run (this same morning) with `fatal: cannot change to '/opt/data/home/.hermes/triaperformance-docs': No such file or directory`. Root cause, confirmed by reading Hermes's own scheduler source (`/opt/hermes/cron/scheduler.py`, `hermes_constants.py`) rather than guessing: in this Docker deployment `HERMES_HOME=/opt/data`, and Hermes correctly finds/runs job scripts at `HERMES_HOME/scripts` (i.e. the dispatcher was in the right place, `/opt/data/scripts/twenty_followup_check.py` — host path `~/.hermes/scripts/...`). But when Hermes spawns the subprocess to *execute* that script, it deliberately sets the subprocess's OS `$HOME` to `{HERMES_HOME}/home` (`/opt/data/home`) — intentionally separate from `HERMES_HOME` itself, so Hermes's own state doesn't collide with whatever a script expects at `~`. The dispatcher's `REPO_DIR = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/triaperformance-docs")` therefore resolved to `/opt/data/home/.hermes/triaperformance-docs` at run time — a path that was never cloned — instead of the one true repo mirror that already exists (and that `deploy-website.sh`'s cron keeps in sync) at `/opt/data/triaperformance-docs`. Fix: changed `REPO_DIR` in the dispatcher to the hardcoded absolute path `"/opt/data/triaperformance-docs"`, removing the `~` expansion entirely. Confirmed working with a real run (`HOME=/opt/data/home python3 /opt/data/scripts/twenty_followup_check.py` inside the `hermes-gateway` container, matching Hermes's actual subprocess env) — produced a clean digest and, being a real (non-dry-run) execution, actually advanced `whatsappTouchCount`/`lastTouchpoint` for the 4 flagged leads. **`update_lead_status.py`'s dispatcher was checked and does NOT have this bug** (no `~`-based path in it). **Standing practice update:** any dispatcher or script invoked via Hermes's `no_agent` cron mechanism must use an absolute path (e.g. `HERMES_HOME`-relative or hardcoded) for anything touching the repo mirror or other Hermes-managed paths — never `os.path.expanduser("~/...")` — because the subprocess's OS `$HOME` is not `HERMES_HOME` and is not the same `$HOME` a plain host shell or host crontab sees.*

*Update, August 12, 2026 — **the audit is now a script: `automation/audit-runtime-paths.sh`.** Written after the auth service became the fourth instance (§13, Aug 10) and it became clear the recurring cost is not the fixing, it is the *asking*: §18 migrated the three scripts it knew about, and the auth service was missed because a reference copy in the repo is indistinguishable from a source copy until someone checks what the runtime loads. The script asks that question mechanically, of four runtime classes: compose-managed containers (via `com.docker.compose.*` labels and bind mounts), cron lines, fixed-path scripts in `~/.hermes/scripts` and friends, and systemd `ExecStart` targets. Read-only — it inspects and prints, changes nothing, safe on the live box at any time.*

*Two limits worth stating rather than discovering. **(1) A container started with a bare `docker run` records no source path anywhere** — `tp-admin` is one — so the image is a black box with respect to where its code came from. Those are reported as CHECK, not passed; they genuinely require a human to remember which Dockerfile was built. **(2) It proves the path, not the revision.** A cron line pointing into the clone without a `git pull` runs whatever the box last happened to fetch, which is a different bug with the same smell; those are flagged separately.*

*Three real defects were found in the script by testing it against a fixture rather than by reading it, which is the note's actual point. **(a)** Every counter was being incremented inside a piped `while` loop — a subshell — so the totals were discarded and the audit reported `outside: 0` regardless of what it found. The worst available failure mode for this particular script, and invisible until a fixture with known-bad files existed. Fixed with process substitution. **(b)** Dispatcher detection searched for the literal string `git pull` and so flagged a known-good dispatcher as an orphan, because the real ones invoke it as `["git", "pull", "-q"]` through subprocess. Now requires two independent signals: a git-pull in any form, and a reference to the clone. **(c)** `find -type f` silently skipped a fixed path that was a symlink into the clone — a legitimate arrangement, never audited. Now `find -L`. *A diagnostic that under-reports is worse than no diagnostic, because it converts "unknown" into "confirmed clean".* Verify any future change to this script against a fixture containing at least one known orphan.*

## 19. Artifact → members-area publishing pipeline (established August 1, 2026)

First run of a now-standing process: interactive training artifacts (Claude-built HTML tools) get published into the gated members area following a fixed checklist, instead of the ad-hoc port that produced the kettlebell outlier (own inline nav, own design language, 173 lines of unique CSS). The checklist lives in **`artifact-publish-runbook.md`** (repo root, same family as the other runbooks).

First artifact through the pipeline: **Activación de Running** (`/members/activacion/`) — guided 8-exercise circuit, 40s work / 15s transition timer, floor-to-standing ordering, unilateral exercises run 40s per side, controls for previous/skip/replace-with-variant. Files: `site/members/activacion/index.njk` (markup + inline JS wrapped in `{% raw %}`), `site/assets/css/members-activacion.css` (self-contained; loads on top of `site.css` only, deliberately not `members.css`), card + new "Activación" filter chip in `site/members/index.njk`.

Decisions codified this session:

- **Dark is the default theme for members-area interactive artifacts** — token set documented in `brand-guidelines.md` §7.1 (added this session), matching `members-home.css`. Static guide pages stay light until unified. Public site stays white-first.
- **Artifacts use the standard `members-page` nav variant and `members` footer variant** (inherited from `members.json`), restyled dark in the page's own CSS — never a bespoke inline nav (the kettlebell mistake).
- **No auth work needed per artifact** — Caddy's `forward_auth` gates `/members/*` by wildcard; a new folder under `site/members/` is born gated.
- **No analytics work needed per artifact** — GA4 + Clarity + noindex arrive via `base.njk` + `members.json`. Verified in the real build output for `/members/activacion/` (both tags present, `noindex` set, absent from sitemap.xml).

Build verified clean with Eleventy v3.1.6 (355 files). Deploy is Iván's commit/push + the VPS cron pull, per standing practice.

*Update, August 1, 2026 (same day) — running tool confirmed deployed live by Iván. Second artifact through the pipeline, this time end-to-end without an approval gate as a process test: **Activación de Ciclismo** (`/members/activacion-ciclismo/`) — same circuit engine, cycling-specific selection (glute bridge, dead bug, open book, clamshell, cat-cow, low-lunge hip-flexor mobility, bodyweight squat, lateral leg swings; suelo → arrodillado → de pie). Reuses `members-activacion.css` wholesale rather than forking it — codified in the runbook as "same tool type = same stylesheet." Card added under the existing `activacion` chip. Build re-verified (356 files: GA4+Clarity ×2, noindex, out of sitemap, raw tags consumed). Pending Iván's commit/push.*

*Update, August 1, 2026 (later same day) — v1 UI rendered poorly on mobile, so both tools were rebuilt on a **3-tab template (v2)** adapted from two Claude-artifact prototypes Iván supplied (Inicio / Rutina / Ejercicios tabs, SVG ring timer, full-screen rest overlay, coaching-cue box per exercise, next-up preview, `navigator.vibrate` on block changes), re-skinned to brand §7.1 (Helvetica, single blue accent instead of the prototypes' per-phase color palette, no serif, no emojis, voseo ES) and keeping the variant-swap feature the prototypes lacked. Architecture changed from per-page inline engines to shared pieces: `site/_includes/partials/activation-tool.njk` (skeleton) + `site/assets/js/activation-tool.js` (engine reading `window.ACTIVATION_DATA`) + `site/assets/css/members-activacion.css` (rewritten) — each tool page is now front matter + a data object + one include. Data model includes long coaching cues, equipment tags, per-variant cues, and a `video` field (YouTube ID) that auto-renders an embed in the Ejercicios tab when populated — ready for Iván's future channel videos, nothing to build later. Verified beyond static grep checks this time: full jsdom smoke test of the built page drove the engine through start → work → rest → unilateral side 1/2 → prev → variant swap → skip-to-done → stats → repeat → list rendering, all correct. Cards on `/members/` unchanged (same URLs). Pending Iván's commit/push + a real phone check.*

*Update, August 1, 2026 (third artifact, same day) — **Core Sin Excusas** (`/members/core/`), a ~15-minute no-equipment hotel-room core routine, published as the first pure data-only page on template v2: no new CSS, no new JS, just `ACTIVATION_DATA` (12 exercises / 13 blocks across sentado → boca arriba → de costado → boca abajo → cuadrupedia, using the new `workSeconds: 45` override — ≈13 min of timed work, ~15 real with position changes) + the shared include + a card under the existing `fuerza` chip. One template improvement while at it: the partial's breadcrumb label was hardcoded "Activación", now data-driven (`crumb` field, added to all three tools). jsdom smoke test run end-to-end (start → 13 blocks → done overlay with correct stats). Pending Iván's commit/push.*

## 20. Content engine — publish path closed and both agents put on cron (August 4, 2026)

The content engine ran end to end for the first time today: an idea the research agent proposed became a drafted article, a reviewed approval, a GitHub commit, a build and a live page — plus its two language siblings. Nine pieces exist, five are live. What follows is the half that didn't exist this morning.

**`automation/publish-article-workflow.json` — the n8n workflow that commits an approved draft.** Iván approves at `/admin/drafts/`, the admin service POSTs to `PUBLISH_WEBHOOK`, and this workflow writes the `.njk` to GitHub via the Contents API and marks the piece `PUBLISHED`. The daily `deploy-website.sh` cron takes it from there — the workflow never touches the live server.

Decisions codified:

- **n8n holds the only GitHub write token** (fine-grained PAT, `Contents: Read and write`, one repo). The VPS deliberately still has no push access — the diverged-branch failure in `website-build-cutover-runbook.md` is why. Rejected alternative: give the VPS a deploy key and `pull/commit/push`. Same outcome, but it makes the box author commits again, and conflict resolution on an unattended cron is how you lose an article. The Contents API is one atomic "put this file at this path" — no working copy, no merge.
- **Articles go through git rather than straight to the webroot** because `deploy-website.sh` does `reset --hard origin/main` + `clean -fd` — a file written directly to the VPS clone survives until the next deploy and then vanishes. An article is source code for the site; if it exists only on the server it has no history, no diff and no revert.
- **Commit straight to `main`, no PR.** Iván already approved the text; a PR is a second approval of the same thing. Consequence he now lives with: origin gains commits he didn't make, so `git pull --rebase` before starting local work is the habit.
- **A failed push does not mark the piece `PUBLISHED`.** The workflow stops at the failed node and the piece stays `APPROVED`, so it gets retried. Marking it published and logging the error would lose articles quietly.
- **Front matter is generated, `lang` and `layout` are not** — those come from each blog directory's `blog.json`. Values are emitted as double-quoted YAML scalars with escaping, verified against a title containing quotes, a colon, an em dash and a leading `-`.

**`automation/content-engine/run-agent.sh` + three crontab lines.** Research Mondays 06:30, writer daily 07:00 (`--limit 3`), auto-translate daily 07:30 (`--limit 2` articles). The `git pull` lives in the crontab line, not the script — a script that updates itself mid-run is still executing the old copy (the stale-deploy-script failure from §18). The wrapper adds a `flock`, a trimmed log, and a status file at `~/.hermes/state/content-<agent>.status` so a cron broken for a fortnight surfaces as something readable rather than as an empty review page that looks like a quiet week. `MAILTO=""` is set, which is what makes that status file load-bearing.

**`writer_agent.py --auto-translate`.** Finds every `parent_id IS NULL` piece in `APPROVED`/`PUBLISHED` missing a language sibling and writes it. Three exclusions, each deliberate: a `DRAFTED` source is one nobody has read, so translating it multiplies an unreviewed opinion by three; a translation of a translation compounds drift and claims the wrong parent; a language that already exists is skipped, so re-running is free — without that check a nightly cron regenerates existing siblings, pays for them, and loses them to `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`.

### The bug worth remembering: n8n's `queryReplacement` splits on commas

Two batch approvals in a row published exactly one of two pieces, silently, with no error and a green execution. Cause: the Postgres node's **Query Parameters field is a comma-separated list**. Passing `"5,4"` intending it as one string for `string_to_array($1, ',')` gave `$1='5'` and `$2='4'`; a query referencing only `$1` fetched one row. The item count was 1 from that node onward, so the Code nodes' `Run Once for Each Item` settings — the thing suspected first, and the documented §18 failure mode — were never involved. `pending_drafts` sorts `generated_at DESC`, which is why the *newer* piece won both times.

Fixed by deleting the parameter: the query now selects `WHERE status = 'APPROVED'`. The webhook became a trigger rather than a payload, which also makes firing it twice harmless and lets a piece stranded by an earlier failure be collected by the next run.

**Standing lesson, third instance this week:** a delimiter-joined string passed through a layer that also splits on that delimiter. Same shape as HTML-inside-a-JSON-string (the writer's first output contract, which died on an unescaped quote 9,500 characters in) and as a Postgres password containing `:` and `@` inside a connection URI. When a value is joined on some character and parsed elsewhere, check who else claims that character.

### Editorial rules added after real output was read

- **Plan spread.** The first drafts linked six marathon plans, all `pace_based`. A reader training by heart rate saw nothing for them. Both prompts now require the linked set to vary on `weeks` and on `metric`.
- **Never change the race in a translation.** The translator adapted "Maratón de Valencia" into "California International Marathon" for the US and "Maratona do Rio" for Brazil, following `MARKET_NOTES`. Iván caught it and was right: an English speaker searching "Valencia marathon training plan" has already entered Valencia — high-intent, low-competition traffic — and swapping in a large domestic race trades it for a keyword contested by every coach in that country. The stronger argument is technical: **hreflang declares these to be the same page translated**, so three different races sharing a `transKey` makes that declaration false. `MARKET_NOTES` race lists are now explicitly examples for articles that name no race, never substitutions. Market notes still adapt price, currency, catalogue scope, units and dialect — they no longer change what the article is about.
- **`validate()` now rejects a plan card whose language doesn't match the article.** A Spanish plan linked from an English article resolves, builds cleanly, and sells nothing — the build guard structurally cannot catch it.
- **Translations are offered topic-matched plans.** `translate()` previously took the first 40 plans in the target language with no filter; for English that's mostly not marathon plans. It now derives the topic from the plan cards the source article used.

**Cost, measured:** ~US$0.08 per original article and ~US$0.05 per translation at `gemini-3.1-pro-preview`, with thinking tokens running 60% of output spend. A full ES→EN→PT set is about US$0.20. The `--limit` flags exist for batch-size sanity, not for the money.

*Note, August 17, 2026 — these figures are still current: **the writer and translator were deliberately left on `gemini-3.1-pro-preview`** in the model migration of that date, while the research agent and Hermes moved to `gemini-3.7-flash`. §33 has the reasoning. If the writer is later moved, the same work costs roughly a third of the above ($0.75/$3.75 per 1M against $2.00/$12.00) — but **cost is explicitly not the argument for moving it**, since $0.20 a set makes the entire annual saving smaller than one month of the VPS.*

*~~Pending: `SETUP.md` still documents `tp-admin` being run with `CONTENT_DB_DSN` (a URI). It is now run with discrete `PG_*` variables — the password contains `:` and `@`, and the URI form is a live tripwire. Correct that file next session.~~*

*Closed August 12, 2026 — `SETUP.md` Steps 3 and 4 both corrected to discrete `PG_*` variables, each with a dated inline note explaining the truncation. **Two places, not one:** the `docker run` line in Step 4 was the known one, but the Step 3 `--dry-run` block carried the same `postgres://` export and would have failed the same way for anyone following the runbook from the top. Confirmed against source rather than assumed: `admin_service/app.py` prefers `CONTENT_DB_DSN` **only when it is set** (`if DSN: return psycopg2.connect(DSN, …)`), falling back to keyword args from `PG_HOST`/`PG_PORT`/`PG_USER`/`PG_PASSWORD`/`PG_DB_CONTENT` — so the fix is to leave the DSN unset, not to override it. `research_agent.py` assembles the same connection from discrete values at `connect()` and reads `~/.analytics/.env` itself when the variables aren't exported.*

*The general rule this is an instance of: **a secret containing `:`, `@`, `/` or `?` cannot go into a connection URI unencoded**, and hand-encoding it is the wrong fix because it puts a mangled secret into shell history. When the library offers a keyword/discrete form, use it. Same family of bug as the `NULLIF`-into-`NOT NULL` trap in §21 — a value that is structurally special to the syntax it's being pasted into.*

### SEO metadata pass (August 6, 2026)

Triggered by an Ahrefs crawl. Measured against the real build before and after, rather than trusting the tool's counts:

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| pages with Open Graph tags | **0 / 374** | 374 |
| pages with Twitter card | **0 / 374** | 374 |
| titles over 60 characters | 355 | 0 |
| longest title | 134 | 60 |
| duplicate titles | 16 | 0 |
| meta descriptions under 110 | 46 | 0 |
| meta descriptions over 160 | 13 | 0 |

**Open Graph mattered most, and not for SEO.** No page had any, so every link shared to WhatsApp — the channel Iván actually sends plan links through — rendered as a bare URL. One templated block in `base.njk` covers all 374 pages and anything added later; blog posts reuse their existing `headline` and get `og:type: article` automatically. A purpose-built 1200×630 card (`og-default.jpg`, 105 KB) replaces what would otherwise have been the hero image. `site.defaultProductImage`, used by the Product schema, pointed at that hero — a **6000×4000, 7.4 MB** file — and now points at the card too.

**Titles: two causes, neither requiring a rename on TrainingPeaks.** The template appended "— Plan de N semanas" to names already beginning "Plan N Semanas", and "| Triaperformance" was appended unconditionally at 18 characters a time. `seoTitleAuto` in `base.njk` now drops the brand suffix when a title doesn't fit and only truncates if it still doesn't — applied globally, so future pages and content-engine articles are covered without anyone remembering.

**Uniqueness had to be solved with the whole catalogue in scope**, not per plan: sibling plans differ in the middle of their names ("Volumen 55-90 km" vs "Intermedio 90-110km"), so truncation collapsed them into identical titles. `plans.js` now truncates, then walks the collisions and re-appends difficulty or week count until each is distinct.

**A real finding fell out of that.** Three pairs of plans carry byte-identical names on TrainingPeaks and are not a titling problem:

- `393663` / `408607` — "Plan 4 Semanas: Natación 1900m - Tu Primer 1.9k (Intermedio)", same price, same difficulty, same length. Two listings for one product, splitting their own sales history.
- `606569` / `606570` — "[Objetivo 2026] Baja de peso con Triatlón + Fuerza en Casa", likewise.
- `612581` / `612820` — "Maratona de Londres 2026 - Corrida + Rotina de Gym - Volume 55-90 km", but **12 weeks at $29.99 vs 18 weeks at $39.99**. Same name, different product, different price — a buyer cannot tell them apart. Worth renaming on TP.

The build prints these on every run, so they can't quietly return.

*Resolved same day, checked against TrainingPeaks by Iván.* `393663` had been carrying `408607`'s name **and** the wrong difficulty: it is the Intermediate "Domina la Distancia", while `408607` is the Beginner "Tus primeros 1900m — Sincroniza con Garmin". `606569`/`606570` are the home-gym and full-gym variants of one weight-loss triathlon plan. `612581`/`612820` were London Marathon 2026, already run, now `is_published=FALSE`. Published count 321 → 319. Correcting the swim difficulty also repaired the sibling cross-links, which had been offering "the Principiante version" of a plan already labelled Principiante.

**A larger finding fell out of the same scan: 17 more published plans are for 2026 races that have already happened** — Tokyo, Barcelona, Boston, London, Santiago and Rio — all still buyable. Cross-checked against `data/races.csv`, which puts the next Boston at 2027-04-19 and records the 2026 Rio edition as run in June. Logged in `open-loops.md`; the recommendation there is to rename to 2027 with corrected start dates rather than unpublish, since only the year is stale and the race-landing-pages initiative will want them.

**Hero image, done the same day.** `hero.jpg` was **6000×4000 and 7.4 MB**, served as the CSS background on every page — a phone was downloading a 24-megapixel photo to fill a 390px box. Replaced with three widths in WebP plus JPEG fallback, selected by width *and* pixel density (a 390px phone at 3× wants ~1170 real pixels, so DPR has to be in the media query or retina screens get a soft image). Layered so it degrades cleanly: a plain `url()` shorthand every browser understands, then `image-set()` upgrades to WebP where supported.

Measured in real Chromium by counting bytes actually transferred:

| client | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| iPhone 13 | 7,236 KB | **72 KB** |
| MacBook 1512 @2× | 7,236 KB | **241 KB** |
| Desktop 1280 @1× | 7,236 KB | **72 KB** |

`hero.jpg` and the unused `hero2.jpg` were deleted — the whole `site/assets/images/` directory is now **1.3 MB**, down from 8.8 MB. Both remain in git history if the 6000px original is ever needed for print.

### Outbound email `From` convention (August 6, 2026)

Every workflow that sends mail uses **`Coach Iván - Triaperformance <coach@triaperformance.com>`** as the `fromEmail`. Iván set this by hand across all live n8n workflows; the reference JSONs in `automation/` were drifting across three different values (`coach@triaperformance.com` bare, `Iván Koch - Triaperformance <…>`, and the correct one) and have been standardised to match. A display name matters more than it looks: a bare address renders as "coach" in most inbox lists, which reads like a shared mailbox rather than a person. Any new email-sending node uses this exact string.

### Churn now sets a status, not just a date (August 6, 2026)

`CHURNED_CUSTOMER` was added to Twenty's `leadStatus` enum (9 values now). Before this, a cancellation set `churnDate` and revoked the member token but left `leadStatus = WON_CUSTOMER`, so **a churned subscriber was indistinguishable from an active one on status alone** — which quietly made the win-back audience unqueryable. The `Mark Churned in Twenty` node's PATCH body now writes both fields. `automation/update_lead_status.py` accepts the value too, with a caveat recorded in the code: its lookup only searches leads at `MESSAGE_SENT`, so a churned subscriber can't actually be found by name through that script yet.

## 21. Athlete intake pipeline — Google Form → Postgres → Gemini briefing (live, August 9, 2026)

The last gap in 1:1 onboarding. An athlete completes the intake form; Iván receives a written briefing before he plans, and the raw answers are stored durably for the first time. Workflow: `automation/athlete-intake-workflow.json` (repo mirror; the n8n instance is the source of truth). Full design record and reasoning: `athlete-onboarding-flow.md` §3.5–3.7.

**Path.** Google Form (one spreadsheet, tabs `ES`/`EN`) → Apps Script `onFormSubmit` → `https://triaperformance.com/api/athlete-intake` → Caddy → n8n webhook → `athlete_intake` table → Gemini → briefing email + Telegram.

- **Apps Script** — `automation/athlete-intake/onFormSubmit.gs`, the source of truth for a file that executes in Google. Same rule as the VPS Python scripts and `Caddyfile` (§18): edit the repo copy, paste to Google, never the reverse. Must be the **installable** spreadsheet trigger — the simple `onFormSubmit()` cannot call external URLs because `UrlFetchApp` requires authorization. One script and one trigger covers both forms, because a spreadsheet-level trigger fires for every form linked to it.
- **Caddy** — `route /api/athlete-intake` with the mandatory `rewrite * /webhook/athlete-intake`, same shape as `/api/contact-form` (§11).
- **Auth** — n8n **Header Auth credential on the webhook node** (`X-Intake-Secret`), not a comparison inside the workflow. A literal secret in an IF node would be committed to this repo the moment the workflow mirror is updated. n8n 401s before any node runs. Trade-off accepted: no alert on rejected attempts.
- **Postgres** — `athlete_intake` in the `members` database. `raw jsonb` holds the complete submission always; typed columns exist only for what automation branches on.
- **Gemini** — `gemini-3.1-pro-preview`, the content engine writer's model, via `x-goog-api-key` Header Auth on the same `GOOGLE_API_KEY` Hermes uses. Pro rather than flash on the strength of lesson 7 below. *(Unchanged in the August 17, 2026 migration — deliberately. It shares the writer's model and should keep sharing it, so it moves when and only when the writer moves. Note this one is hardcoded in the n8n `Build Briefing Prompt` Code node, not read from an env var, so it is Iván's edit in the n8n UI rather than a repo change. §33.)*

**Four things worth carrying forward:**

- **Key form data on question text, never column index.** Google Forms appends new questions as columns at the far right regardless of form order, and deleting a question leaves its column in place. The August 2026 form edit put the two new questions at columns 39–40, after seven now-dead columns. `e.namedValues` delivers a question-text → answer map and is immune to both.
- **A column the database should fill must be omitted from an n8n Postgres mapping, never left blank.** On connecting the credential, n8n auto-populates every column: `id` arrives pre-filled with `0` (overriding the `bigserial` sequence — the second insert collides on the primary key) and `submitted_at` arrives blank, which sends NULL and defeats `DEFAULT now()`. **A default applies only when the column is absent from the INSERT.**
- **Respond to a webhook before slow work, not after.** `Respond OK` sits mid-chain: n8n keeps executing after responding, so Apps Script gets its 200 as soon as the row is durable rather than holding a connection through a 10–30s LLM call.
- **Compute dates in code, not in the prompt.** Athlete age drives a real structural decision (`methodology.md` §5 — 50+ gets 2+1 blocks and shortened ATL/CTL constants), and date arithmetic is what an LLM gets subtly wrong. It's computed in `Build Briefing Prompt` and handed to the model as a number.

**The prompt is condensed from `methodology.md` §2, §3 and §5** — test-protocol table, red-flag list, the 50+ rule. If the methodology changes, `Build Briefing Prompt` is the thing to update. Its load-bearing instructions: never invent (say *"no lo dijo"*), tag every day of the proposed week `[dicho]` or `[inferido]`, and list injuries with *"derivar a médico"* without interpreting them (§11 red line).

*Open, deliberately:* the `athlete_profile` table designed in `athlete-onboarding-flow.md` §2 **was not created** — only `athlete_intake` exists. Building a schema nothing writes to yet would be speculative. It belongs with the perfect-week capture (Stage 8), which is still manual.

*Update, August 20, 2026 — two more lessons of the same kind, both from the coaching branch of `subscription-lifecycle-automation.json`, both found chasing a single misleading error. Full incident in `open-loops.md`.*

- **n8n's Postgres node deletes empty query parameters instead of passing NULL.** In `executeQuery` on node version >= 2.5 each `{{ }}` in **Query Parameters** is resolved, then run through `stringToArray`, which is `String(v).split(',').filter(Boolean)`. `undefined` and `''` therefore contribute **zero** values, the array shortens, and every later parameter shifts left. The error names the **highest** placeholder, not the missing one: a blank `$2` in a four-parameter insert reports **`there is no parameter $4`**. `null` survives -- it arrives as the literal four-character string `'null'`. **Two consequences.** (1) Do not debug the placeholder the error names; read the node's INPUT panel and find the field that is `""`. (2) The dangerous case is *not* the one that errors -- if the shift still leaves a valid count, the node writes the wrong value into the wrong column and reports success. **Any expression in Query Parameters that can resolve empty must carry its own fallback**, e.g. `{{ $json.preferredLanguage || 'UNKNOWN' }}`. Same class as the `NULLIF`-into-`NOT NULL` trap and the blank-`submitted_at` trap above: *absent and empty are different things to every layer here, and each layer disagrees about which one it received.*
- **A TrainingPeaks transactional email is not guaranteed to have a plain-text part.** The CoachMatch hire template (`Congratulations, You Have Been Hired by a New Athlete`) is sent as a **single `text/html` part** -- the IMAP node delivers `"textPlain": ""`. Any parser reading `item.json.textPlain` alone returns nothing, and without a gate the blanks propagate all the way into Twenty. Parsers on this source should prefer `textPlain` and **fall back to stripping and entity-decoding `textHtml`**. Also on that source: `item.json.date` is **RFC-2822** (`Thu, 20 Aug 2026 18:04:37 +0000`), so `.slice(0, 10)` yields `"Thu, 20 Au"` -- parse it through `new Date()` and emit `''` on failure rather than a truncated string. **Neither showed up in twelve days of testing because every test payload was hand-authored, and a hand-written fixture agrees with whatever the code already assumes.** Capture email fixtures from a real IMAP execution's node output.

## 22. Members area in three languages — skeleton shipped (August 10, 2026); ~~shell only~~ **fully translated and confirmed live, August 21, 2026**

Extends §13, which built a single Spanish members area. The gate itself is unchanged; what changed is that there are now three language trees behind it, and which one a subscriber sees is decided by the database rather than by which page they happened to open.

**The structural decision, and the one that everything else follows from: language is a path SEGMENT under `/members/`, not a language prefix.** The public site uses `/en/plans/` and `/pt/planos/`; the members area deliberately does not, and uses `/members/`, `/members/en/`, `/members/pt/` instead. The reason is the gate. A language prefix would mean three separate auth trees in the Caddyfile, and an ordering or typo mistake in any one of them exposes a whole language's paid content silently — there is no error, the page simply serves. One `/members/*` tree cannot fail that way: anything under it that isn't explicitly listed as public is gated by default. The area is `noindex`, so the URL shape costs nothing in SEO, which is the only thing the prefix convention was buying.

**Files:**

- **`site/members/en/` and `site/members/pt/`** — home, `login/`, `forgot-password/`. Each carries a one-line directory data file (`en.json` / `pt.json`) that sets `lang` only; `noindex`, `navVariant`, `footerVariant` and the analytics decision all cascade down from `site/members/members.json`, so a new page in either tree inherits the gate-adjacent settings without anyone remembering to set them.
- **`site/_data/nav.json`** — `membersHome` and `membersPage` were single ES-only objects with a hardcoded `/members/` home; they are now keyed by language. The ES sub-objects are byte-identical to what shipped in July. `nav.njk` resolves off the page's own `lang` with an ES fallback, so a members page with no `lang` renders Spanish rather than failing the build.
- **`site/_includes/partials/footer.njk`** — the `members` footer variant was a hardcoded Spanish string; localised inline.
- **`automation/members-area/auth_service/app.py`** — see below.
- **`automation/Caddyfile`** — see below.

**Login is now routed by the token, not by the page.** All three login pages POST to the same `/members/login` endpoint. On success with no explicit `next`, `app.py` reads the token's `preferred_language` (`SPANISH`/`ENGLISH`/`PORTUGUESE`, cached from Twenty — see `schema.sql`) and sends the subscriber to that language's home. That column has existed since the schema was written in July and this is the first thing that consumes it for anything beyond a debug read. When `next` *is* set, Caddy put it there because the person was trying to reach a specific gated page, and that intent wins over the language default.

The login pages post a hidden `lang` for one narrow purpose: deciding which login screen a **failed** attempt lands back on. On a failed login there is no valid token, so `preferred_language` is unavailable — the only thing we know is which page they were reading. Same field on the logout form in the members-home nav, for the same reason.

**Caddy: three gates, ordered.** `/members/en/*` and `/members/pt/*` are declared **before** the catch-all `/members/*`; they are identical apart from the login page an unauthenticated visitor is bounced to. Get the order wrong and they never match — the failure mode is not an error but every gated EN/PT athlete landing on the Spanish login screen. Anything under `/members/` that is neither `en/` nor `pt/` falls through to the ES gate, which is the correct default: a new subtree is gated, not exposed.

Two things worth writing down about the public-page matcher:

- **All twelve public paths go on ONE `path` line.** Caddy rejects the same matcher token appearing twice inside a single named matcher set, so splitting them across three readable lines fails to parse rather than doing what it looks like it does.
- **Paths are listed explicitly, never wildcarded.** A wildcard typo here is the difference between "a page is public" and "paid content is public". Explicit paths cannot fail that way, and the verbosity is the point.
