**`next` is now validated.** It arrives from a query string via a hidden form field, so it is attacker-controlled in the ordinary sense: without a check, a crafted link could bounce a subscriber to any host immediately after a successful login, wearing our own domain in the address bar on the way. `safe_next()` in `app.py` accepts only same-site absolute paths and rejects the protocol-relative forms (`//host`, `/\host`); the client-side JS on each login page applies the same rule before filling the field. This was not introduced by this change — the previous version passed `next` through unchecked — but it is fixed here rather than left.

**Public nav: a "Members Access" button in all three languages** (`member: true` in `nav.json` → `.nav-member`). Deliberately a ghost/outline button rather than a second solid one: the nav already carries the blue Contacto CTA, and two filled buttons side by side read as two equally-weighted asks. It points at the language's members **home**, not its login page — a subscriber with a live cookie walks straight in, and everyone else is bounced to the right-language login by the gate. Linking straight to `/login` would show a login form to someone already logged in.

**Known gap, not fixed here:** the `Webhook - Resend Password` branch of `subscription-lifecycle-automation.json` (§13) sends a Spanish recovery email regardless of who asks. The three forgot-password pages now send `lang` in the JSON body so the workflow *can* read it, but the workflow does not yet. Until it is wired up in the n8n UI, an EN or PT athlete who recovers a password receives Spanish copy. Logged in `open-loops.md` — do not assume it is done because the field is being sent.

**Verified live, August 10, 2026**, from the VPS against the real stack — not read off the code. Six checks, all passing: `/members/`, `/members/en/` and `/members/pt/` each 302 to their own language's login with `next` preserved (proving the gate ordering took); a wrong password with `lang=pt` and `lang=en` returns to the matching login with `?error=1`; and a real PT token with no `next` lands on `/members/pt/`. That last one is the only check that proves `preferred_language` is being read rather than everyone quietly defaulting to Spanish, which is what a code review alone would have missed. The reusable form of the check is in the deploy log for this date; `deploy-website.sh` now also asserts a 200 on all three login paths on every deploy, since a drifted public-path list produces a locked-out subscriber and no error anywhere.

**Content status:** the shell is built and empty on purpose. No tools exist in EN or PT; the library section renders a designed empty state rather than cards pointing at the Spanish pages, which would set the expectation that the site is translated when it isn't. The zone calculator is the first tool going in, in all three languages at once.

*Update, August 21, 2026 — superseded. All tool artifacts originally published in Spanish (the zone calculator, activation matrix, core, knees/Achilles, breathing, load) have since been translated into English and Portuguese — see §25 (zone calculator) and §26 (members zones calculator retirement) for the build detail. Iván confirmed all three language trees working correctly, in real use, August 21, 2026. **Do not read this section's "no tools exist in EN or PT" line as current** — it is an accurate record of the day this skeleton shipped, not of where the branch ended. The branch itself closed the same day this note was added; see `open-loops.md`.*

## 23. Zone-calculator layout bugs — the four public pages had three different left edges (August 13, 2026)

Iván's first real look at `/calculadora-de-zonas/*` on desktop Chrome. Three defects, all present since the Aug 10 build, all in CSS. Measured in a real headless Chromium at 1440px before and after — not read off the stylesheet, which is what let the third one hide.

**What was wrong.** On a 1440px viewport the page had three left edges where it should have had one: hero/nav content at **204px**, the calculator at **330px**, the prose sections at **360px**.

1. **`.zc { margin: 0 auto; max-width: 780px }`** — the calculator centred itself inside `.wrap`'s 1032px content box, so its left edge sat 126px right of the hero. Fixed to `margin: 0`. *A `max-width` decides how wide a block is; it should never be what decides where it starts.*
2. **`.wrap.zc-prose { max-width: 720px }`** — this overrode `.wrap`'s 1080px box, and `.wrap`'s own `margin: 0 auto` then centred the narrower box, putting the prose 156px right of the hero. **The first fix for this was wrong**: pinning `margin-left: 0` moved the block flush to the viewport edge (measured L=0), losing the page gutter entirely. Correct fix is to constrain the text and leave the wrap alone — `.zc-prose > * { max-width: 720px }`.
3. **The zone-note row reused `.zc-pct`, which carries `white-space: nowrap`.** Right for `100–102%`, catastrophic for the 172-character Zone X explanation: as a `colspan="3"` cell it set the table's min-content width to **1060px inside a 780px container**, so the table overhung not just its own container but the nav's right edge. This is the one Iván saw as "the table goes beyond Contacto". Now its own class, `.zc-zone-note`.

**Two follow-on bugs found only by looking at a screenshot, not by measuring.** The note cell is a `colspan` cell and therefore also the row's *last* cell, so it inherited `.zc-table td:last-child { text-align: right }` and rendered ragged-left — invisible for as long as it was `nowrap` on a single line, and invisible to a width assertion, which is why the first "fix" measured clean and still looked wrong. And the corrected rule then *silently lost on specificity*: `.zc-table td:last-child` scores (0,2,1) against `.zc-table .zc-zone-note`'s (0,2,0). The selector needs the element — `td.zc-zone-note` — to tie and win on source order.

***The process lesson, which cost three round trips: I twice wrote an explanatory comment by inserting text after a block comment's closing `*/`, producing stray tokens that made the browser drop the rule that followed.*** The rule was in the file, read correctly, and did nothing. Both times the symptom was identical to a specificity problem, which sent the next fix in the wrong direction. **A CSS rule being present in the source is not evidence it is applied.** The check that actually discriminates is reading `getComputedStyle` for the property in question — added to the assertion script below, and the reason the real cause was found on the third pass instead of the sixth.

**How it was verified.** A Playwright script drives each page to its results state (pick sport → protocol if any → fill inputs → calculate) and asserts, per page per viewport: hero/calculator/table/capture share one left edge; table width ≤ container width; the note's computed `text-align` is `left` and `white-space` is `normal`; no document-level horizontal overflow. **5 pages × 3 viewports (390 / 768 / 1440), all passing** — the four public pages plus the gated members copy, which shares the component and would otherwise have been assumed rather than checked. Chromium in this sandbox needs `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` pointing at a stub `libXdamage.so.1` (no root to install it); on a normal machine `npx playwright install chromium` is enough.

**Standing note for this stylesheet:** the calculator ships into both a light public page and a dark members page from one component, so any layout change here must be checked on `/members/calculadora-de-zonas/` too, not only on the public four.

*Update, same day — a second round on Iván's review, and the more interesting one, because this defect was not visual.* **On a sport-locked page the sport picker was still live**, so switching to cycling on `/calculadora-de-zonas/running/` produced bike zones sitting under the 30-minute *run*-test explanation. The page contradicted itself and nothing about it looked broken. Cause: the component was built before the three sport pages existed, when "always let them switch" was correct; `zcSport` only pre-selected. Now it locks — the sport step renders `hidden`, `.zc` carries `data-lock`, and the recalculate path re-opens the inputs without re-opening the picker (the same bug's second door, and the one a load-time-only check would have missed). The hub and the members copy keep the free picker: neither wraps the tool in sport-specific prose. Decision and the rule behind it: `zones-calculator-brief.md` decision 21. Nav in the same pass: the `Recursos` dropdown now indents the three sport calculators under "Calculadora de Zonas" (`sub: true` in `nav.json` → `.nav-sub`) instead of listing five equal-looking items — *the four URLs were never the duplication problem; a flat list that refused to say how they relate was.*

**The check is now a file, not a session's scratch script: `automation/layout-check.js`** (120 assertions covering layout *and* the sport lock, exits non-zero, runnable before any deploy that touches this component). It follows §18's rule that anything worth running twice lives in the repo. It needs `npx playwright install chromium` once. *Nothing invokes it automatically yet — it is a command Iván or a session runs, not a cron job or a deploy gate. Making it one is a decision, not an oversight.*

## 24. Zone-calculator lead magnet — guide built and delivery pipeline spec'd (August 13, 2026)

The calculator's email capture has been posting to `/api/zone-workouts` since Aug 10 with nothing listening. Two halves shipped today: the artefact it promises, and the pipeline that delivers it.

**The artefact.** `site/assets/guias/sesiones-por-zona.pdf` — 16 pages, natación + ciclismo + running, generated by `automation/build-lead-magnet-pdf.js` (branded HTML → headless Chromium). Content home doc: `lead-magnet-sesiones-por-zona.md`. *Generated rather than hand-made on purpose: the zone percentages in it are copies of `data/zones.csv`, and a drifted copy of those numbers is precisely what the same day was spent correcting in the source drafts.*

**The pipeline.** `automation/zone-workouts-workflow.json`, cloned from `plan-lead-workflow.json` rather than authored fresh — same three credentials, same duplicate-detection branch, same Twenty shape. Caddy route added to `automation/Caddyfile` alongside `/api/plan-lead`, including the mandatory internal `rewrite` to `webhook/`. Full deploy steps: `zone-magnet-runbook.md`.

**Two deliberate divergences from the plan-catalog pipeline**, both Iván's call:

- **`excludeFromSequence: true`** (plan-catalog uses `false`). A plan-catalog lead asked for help choosing a plan, which is a request to be contacted; this one asked for a free PDF. The sell still happens — the delivery email and the guide's last page both carry the All-Access CTA — it just happens once, honestly.
- **Delivery is a link, not an attachment**, matching the three existing lead magnets. The guide can be corrected without stale copies in inboxes, and the click is a signal. Accepted trade-off: the URL is public.

**Verified before handover, in a real browser rather than by reading the code:** the capture POSTs the documented payload to the documented path, renders the thank-you only on a 2xx, and on a 404 shows its error rather than a false confirmation. The workflow JSON was structurally validated — every connection endpoint and every `$('Node')` reference resolves to a real node, which a hand-edited clone silently breaks when a node gets renamed. **What could not be verified from here and is explicitly Iván's:** the Twenty enum, the Caddy reload, and the n8n import — the standing division of labour, not a gap.

***One correction the build forced, worth recording because it was a promise not a bug:*** the capture's fine print said "las sesiones por zona **para el deporte que elegiste**" while the guide covers all three sports. Rewritten in `zonesUi.json` to promise the full guide. *A microcopy line written months before the artefact existed had quietly become a claim the artefact would contradict on sight.*

## 25. Zone calculator and lead magnet in three languages — the i18n branch's definition of done (August 13, 2026)

The branch opened Aug 10 with one stated definition of done: *the zone calculator live and working in all three languages.* Delivered, plus the guide behind it.

**What shipped.** `zonesUi.json` gained full `en` and `pt` blocks — ~35 keys each, every string in the tool. Eight new pages on **localised slugs** (`/en/training-zones-calculator/{,swimming,cycling,running}/` and `/pt/calculadora-de-zonas/{,natacao,ciclismo,corrida}/`), each with its own directory data file and its own coaching prose below the tool, not a translated hero over shared body copy. `transKey` values match their Spanish siblings, so hreflang wired itself across all twelve pages the moment they built. The guide became three PDFs, one per language, each linking its **own** checkout product and language home.

**The build script was split, not duplicated.** `build-lead-magnet-pdf.js` is now template and layout only; every word a reader sees moved to `automation/lead-magnet-content.js`, keyed by language. `node automation/build-lead-magnet-pdf.js en` builds the English guide. A fourth language is one key, and the builder is untouched. *The alternative — three near-identical build scripts — is the same drift this repo has a rule against, just expressed in code instead of prose.*

**Three things that had been deliberately withheld were released in the same pass**, which is what "in the same pass" in the Aug 10 and Aug 12 notes was reserving:

- The EN and PT article CTAs, stripped on Aug 12 rather than point an English reader at a Spanish calculator, now point at their own.
- EN and PT nav gained the `Recursos` dropdown, held back because it would have contained a single item.
- The n8n workflow stopped hardcoding the Spanish PDF. **This was a live time bomb, not a nicety**: the workflow already mapped `en`/`pt` and already had English and Portuguese email copy, so the first English lead after the EN calculator shipped would have received a fluent English email linking a Spanish PDF — and nothing anywhere would have reported an error.

**Two verification notes worth keeping.** First, the eight new pages initially rendered with no `<html>`, no `<head>` and no analytics, because the Spanish pages get `layout` from a directory data file rather than front matter and I copied the front matter, not the directory file. *Caught by grepping the built HTML for `<html lang=`, not by looking at the pages — they looked fine.* Second, `layout-check.js` at 13 pages × 3 viewports now takes longer than a single sandbox call allows, so it gained `LAYOUT_CHECK_ONLY` and `LAYOUT_CHECK_VIEWPORTS`. **A check that cannot be run in one sitting becomes a check nobody runs**, which is worse than a slower check that gets scoped. All twelve public pages plus the members copy pass.

## 26. Members zones calculator — the two guide pages retired into it (August 13, 2026)

Decision 16 in `zones-calculator-brief.md` parked the retirement of `/members/zonas/` and `/members/tests/` with an explicit trigger: *build the calculator first, then look at what actually overlaps.* The trigger fired. Both pages are gone; `/members/calculadora-de-zonas/` absorbed what was worth keeping.

**What moved, and what did not.** The test-validity rules and the testing log moved — those were the only things on `/members/tests/` the calculator could not already do, and the rules in particular are load-bearing: an invalid test does not shift one zone, it shifts all seven by the same margin. The `/members/zonas/` model explanation moved in a compressed form. The step-by-step "how to do the test" content did **not** move: the calculator already renders it per protocol, from the same data.

**Redirects, not deletions.** `automation/Caddyfile` sends both old paths (with and without trailing slash) to the calculator, `permanent`. These URLs sit in subscribers' bookmarks and in at least one sent email.

**Three differences from the public copy**, all deliberate: dark chrome via `members-zones-calculator.css`; no email capture; and in the capture's slot, a direct guide download — a subscriber has already paid for that PDF, and asking for an email to hand it over would charge them twice, once in money and once in friction. Plan recommendations now reveal at calculate time there too, rather than after a form that does not exist.

***Three bugs found in this build, in ascending order of how well they hid:***

**1. The page shipped with no component stylesheet.** Rewriting the front matter replaced a two-entry `pageCss` list with one entry and dropped `zones-calculator.css`. Every structural assertion still passed — right elements, right ids, right widths reported — and the page was visibly broken. *Caught only by looking at a screenshot.* `layout-check.js` gained a `component styled` assertion that reads a computed background colour, because "the rule is in the file" and "the file is loaded" are different claims.

**2. The members-only guide block was nested inside `{% if zcCapture !== false %}`** — the branch that by definition excludes members. It rendered for nobody. The `if` also wrapped the plans block, so lifting the guide out required splitting one conditional into two, which is why the plans now render for members as well.

**3. Every dark members page was horizontally scrollable by a full screen on a phone — pre-existing, not introduced here.** `.site-nav-sticky` carries `backdrop-filter`, and a backdrop-filter makes an element the containing block for fixed-position descendants. That converts the closed slide-out menu from viewport-fixed (contributes no scrollable overflow) into an out-of-flow child of the header, so its `right: -100%` parking spot starts counting toward document width. The public pages have identical nav markup, no backdrop-filter, and no overflow. **Fixed by dropping the blur under 860px**, which restores the public behaviour exactly and costs nothing visually — the bar is already 85% opaque. *Three earlier attempts failed and are recorded in the stylesheet so they are not repeated: `overflow-x: clip` on `body` (propagates to the viewport and clips nothing), the same on `html` (html is not the containing block), and parking the menu with `transform: translateX(100%)` (transformed boxes still count toward scrollable overflow).* The same fix went into `members-activacion.css`, which had the identical defect on the three activation tools.

***The through-line in all three: an assertion that passes is not a page that works.*** Two of them were invisible to every check in the repo and visible in one screenshot; the third was invisible in a screenshot and only showed up when the check asked whether the page could actually be scrolled, rather than what its `scrollWidth` reported.

*Update, same day — Iván's review of the finished page, six changes.* **The rescued prose came straight back out**, on both the members page and the three public hubs. Under a working tool it read as unlinked filler answering questions the tool already answers where they are actually asked — the protocol step explains how to test, the zone notes explain X and Y. *Removed rather than commented out: a dead 90-line block in a template is what a future session quotes back as current.* **The SEO trade-off is deliberate and worth re-reading before anyone "fixes" it**: the three hubs now carry almost no body copy, and the hub is the page targeting the head term. The sport pages keep their prose and are the stronger ranking surfaces; if copy returns to a hub it should be written for someone who has already used the tool.

Also: the guide block's title lost its joke (*"sin pedirte nada"* referred to a contrast — the public version's email form — that a subscriber has never seen, so it read as a non-sequitur); `.zc a` now takes the theme accent, because without it links fell through to the browser's default blue, which on `#0d1117` was effectively unreadable and was how the plan recommendations shipped; and `membersPage` lost its "Área de Miembros" item in all three languages — the logo in the same bar already went there, so the bar had two links plus a breadcrumb pointing at one destination.

*Update, same day — members calculator now in three languages, plus one self-inflicted regression.* `/members/en/training-zones-calculator/` and `/members/pt/calculadora-de-zonas/` shipped, and the EN/PT members homes lost their "library is on its way" empty state for a real card. The partial picks the right guide PDF from `lang`, so each language downloads its own.

***The regression is the instructive part: fixing link contrast broke button contrast.*** The `.zc a` rule added an hour earlier scores (0,2,0) and `.zc-btn` only (0,1,0), so the download button — an `<a>` carrying `.zc-btn` — had its label repainted in the accent colour, on top of an accent-coloured background. Invisible in every existing assertion and invisible in the screenshot I took, because that screenshot was framed on the plan links the rule was added to fix. Now `.zc a:not(.zc-btn)`, with a `button label readable` assertion in `layout-check.js` that compares the computed label colour against the computed background. **A component-wide element selector will find things that are not what you were thinking about — buttons, breadcrumbs, anything that happens to be an anchor.**

## 27. Members library pruned, and the downloads page went dark (August 13, 2026)

Iván's call, in one pass: retire `/members/carrera/` (race pacing) and `/members/kettlebell/`, retire the `zonas-de-entrenamiento.pdf` card from `/members/guias/`, add the new zone-sessions guide in its place, and open EN/PT downloads pages.

**Content deliberately not moved anywhere.** The pacing guide is simply gone. The kettlebell routine was English copy sitting on the Spanish members area with its own inline nav and 173 lines of unique CSS — it has been the named counter-example in the artifact runbook since §20, and Iván will rebuild it rather than port it. *Both directories were deleted, not redirected: they are `noindex` and gated, so the only traffic that can reach them is a subscriber's own bookmark, and a 404 tells that subscriber the truth.* **This also closes the "kettlebell keeps its own inline nav" item that had been carried since the nav unification — it closed by deletion, which is a legitimate way for a cosmetic item to close.**

**The retired guide's file is still on disk, on purpose.** `/blog/como-elegir-tu-plan-de-maraton/` links it publicly, and there are three Caddy redirects (§16) serving it to already-sent email sequences. Removing the card retires it from the members area; it does not retire it from the internet. *Raised as a gap and closed the same session: **Iván's decision is that the file stays served, blog link and redirects included.** Retiring a card is a curation decision about the paid library — a guide can be too weak to headline the members area and still beat nothing for a cold reader with no zones at all. Revisit when the replacement exists, not before.*

**`members-downloads.css` — the fourth dark members stylesheet, and the second attempt at this page.** The first attempt at a dark `/members/guias/` (§19, July 28) reached for `members-home.css` and produced dark content under a white nav, because `members-home.css` themes `.site-nav` (the home's variant) and every subpage uses `.site-nav-sticky`. That attempt was reverted to light. The fix this time is the same one the activation and breathing tools use: a page-scoped stylesheet that themes `.site-nav-sticky` itself, including the `backdrop-filter` mobile-overflow fix from §26. **The nav variant is what decides which selector to theme — not the page's role.**

The EN and PT pages each hold exactly one guide, with a line saying more are coming. Same reasoning as the empty-library state those homes shipped with in §22: the other four PDFs are Spanish-only, and a translated card pointing at a Spanish file is worse than a short honest list.

## 28. `/members/carga/` rewritten from Iván's TrainingPeaks metrics doc (August 13, 2026)

Source: `Metricas de training peaks.md`, written by Iván and handed over to replace the thinner original page. **The source file was deliberately not committed to the repo.** The page is now the home doc for this content; a second copy in Markdown is the exact shape of drift the hygiene rules exist to prevent.

**Four corrections made on the way in, and one dead link fixed.**

1. **The TSB bands overlapped.** "Mantenimiento −10 a +10" and "Puesta a punto +5 a +20" both claimed +5…+10, so an athlete at +7 got two answers. *Iván's call was to keep the overlap and explain it* — the same +7 is maintenance or race-ready depending on the race and, more importantly, on which direction it is travelling. A +7 rising from −15 is a taper working; a +7 falling from +25 is someone coming back. **The page now says the direction matters more than the band**, which is a better answer than either clean split would have been.
2. **CTL and ATL were described as simple averages.** They are exponentially weighted. This is not pedantry: the weighting is the reason one enormous session barely moves CTL and six consistent weeks do, which is the single most common misunderstanding of the chart.
3. **The 42/7-day windows were stated as universal.** They are TrainingPeaks defaults, and `methodology.md` §blocks shortens them deliberately for athletes 50+. The page now says so, which also quietly explains to a 50+ athlete why their numbers move differently from a training partner's.
4. **"El TSS premia exponencialmente la intensidad"** — it is quadratic, and the doc's own three worked examples prove it (halving IF quadruples the time needed). Corrected to "al cuadrado", with the consequence spelled out.

**Dead link, found and fixed:** the page's closing paragraph pointed at `/members/zonas/`, retired into the calculator earlier the same day (§26). It resolved only because of the Caddy redirect, under link text naming a page that no longer exists. *A redirect is not a working link — it is a working URL under a wrong promise.* Now points at the calculator, and the threshold warning at the top links both the calculator and `/blog/entrenar-el-umbral-sin-tiras-de-lactato/`.

**Two images, both Iván's real data** (`/assets/images/pmc-ejemplo.jpg`, `/assets/images/tp-fitness-fatiga-forma.jpg`), added because he shares this URL with athletes and the screenshots are what make them recognise their own app. ***The phone screenshot earns its own section: it shows Fitness 91, Fatiga 96, Forma 13 — and 91 − 96 is not 13.*** TrainingPeaks shows today's CTL and ATL beside *yesterday's* TSB, which is correct (the form you start the day with was set before you trained) and looks like a bug to every athlete who does the subtraction. That section exists because the question is unavoidable once the image is on the page.

**One new figure with no prior owner: the 3–7 CTL/week ramp.** Iván chose the standard industry range rather than publishing his own. It is framed on the page as a general guideline for reading your own chart, explicitly not a prescription, with a line saying the ramp is already built into his plans. *If a Triaperformance-specific number ever replaces it, this page is where it lives — do not restate it elsewhere.*

*Update, same day — translated and published in EN and PT.* `/members/en/training-load/` and `/members/pt/carga-de-treino/`, plus a card on each home. The two screenshots are shared across all three languages rather than recaptured: they are Iván's own TrainingPeaks account, the app's own UI is in English, and the numbers are the point. **Only the alt text and captions are translated** — an EN reader sees an English description of a screen they can already read, and a PT reader gets the labels named in Portuguese beside the English UI, which is what they will actually see when they open their own app.

*Each language links its own siblings*: the threshold warning points at `/en/blog/threshold-training-without-lactate-strips/` + `/members/en/training-zones-calculator/`, and at `/pt/blog/treinar-o-limiar-sem-tiras-de-lactato/` + `/members/pt/calculadora-de-zonas/`. **This is the failure that bit the lead-magnet emails in §25** — per-language content assembled with one language's links still embedded — so it was checked rather than assumed: every internal link on all three pages was requested in a real browser and returned 200.

**Mobile table bug, pre-existing and inherited.** `.tp-table` rendered 399px wide inside a 342px column at 390px, scrolling the whole page sideways — present on the old page too. Fixed two ways because the tables fail differently: the TSS table only needed smaller type, while the TSB and taper tables carry a sentence per cell and now stack into one card per row under 640px, reprinting the column header from `data-label`. *No font size rescues three columns of prose on a phone.*

## 29. The activation matrix — v1, and the two fixed tools retired (August 13, 2026)

Design doc: `activation-matrix.md`. Recovered into the repo the same day, having existed only as a chat upload.

**Shape.** `/members/activacion/` asks three questions — sport, how you arrive (just woke / sat all day), mini-band or not — and builds one of 8 routines. `/members/activacion-ciclismo/` is deleted; the running tool that lived at this URL is replaced in place. **Deleted rather than redirected**, same reasoning as `carrera` and `kettlebell` in §27: gated, `noindex`, and the running URL is the one that survives anyway.

**The engine was not modified to support this**, which is the part worth copying next time. The page renders its own setup screen, builds `window.ACTIVATION_DATA` from the answers, and *then* injects `activation-tool.js` — so by the time the engine runs it sees an ordinary activation tool and needs no matrix awareness. The only shared change is one flag in `partials/activation-tool.njk` (`matrixMode`) suppressing its own boot script. *An engine that has to know about every page that uses it stops being an engine.*

**Data shape: one library, eight lists of ids.** The 8 routines share about a dozen exercises. Written out as 8 hand-authored routines, those dozen cues become a dozen slightly different cues within two edits — the same drift the zone tables produced across four documents. One `L` object, one cue per exercise, and the routines reference ids.

**Bug found and fixed in the shared engine:** `#changeLink` ("Cambiar ejercicio") rendered unconditionally while its handler already no-ops for an exercise with no variants. Invisible until now because every previous routine had variants everywhere; the matrix's v1 has none. **A control that renders, invites a tap and does nothing is worse than an absent one** — mid-set, the athlete concludes the tool is broken. Now hidden when there is nothing to swap to. Verified the three `core` pages, which do have variants, still show it.

*Update, same day — EN and PT shipped, and a bug the translation exposed.* `/members/en/activation/` and `/members/pt/ativacao/`, cards on both homes. All 8 combinations exercised in a real browser on each of the three pages, 24 runs.

***The translation surfaced that the activation engine was hardcoded Spanish.*** `/members/en/core/` and `/members/pt/core/` had been live since Aug 12 with **Spanish tabs, buttons and labels wrapped around correctly translated exercises** — `Inicio / Rutina / Ejercicios`, `Empezar activación →`, `Por qué importa`, `alternado · 45s`, `Descanso`, `Cambiar ejercicio`. The content was translated; the interface around it never was, because nobody had looked at the *chrome* — only at the words the page supplied. **Fixed by `site/_data/activationUi.json`** (es/en/pt), read by the partial for server-rendered strings and passed to the engine as `window.ACTIVATION_UI` for runtime ones. Same pattern and same reason as `zonesUi.json`: the page owns the content, the data file owns the interface, and a fourth language is one object rather than a fork of the engine.

*One string escaped the first pass and was caught by a browser sweep rather than by grep:* `nextBlockInfo()` built `ex.name + " — Lado 2"` inline, so English read `Next: Nerve glide — Lado 2`. **It was invisible to a search for hardcoded strings because I searched the lines I had already decided were UI strings.** The sweep that found it reads every rendered chrome element on the workout, list and done screens and tests them against a Spanish word list — worth keeping as the check for any future language work.

**Two content decisions raised for Iván, both recorded in the home doc:** the "figure 4 right priority / 90 seconds right side" cues were his own asymmetry and were neutralised to "start with the side that feels tighter" rather than prescribing one person's imbalance to every subscriber; and the routines come out at 16–19 minutes against the ~10 of the tools they replace, roughly five of which is rest. *Neither was silently changed in the other direction — a coach's prescription is not a thing to quietly shorten.*

## 30. A second engine — strength artifacts, and `/members/rodillas/` (August 13, 2026)

Home doc: `knee-strength-brief.md`. Also the day `/members/nutricion/` was retired (Iván's instruction, content not moved, filter chip removed, page deleted rather than redirected — same reasoning as §27).

**Why a second engine rather than reusing the activation one.** The knee routine is 3 × 8-12, not a timed circuit. Forcing it onto a 40-second countdown would have been the cheap option and a wrong one: ***a countdown tells an athlete to keep going when the prescription is eight good repetitions.*** `strength-tool.js` inverts the relationship — the athlete finishes the set and taps, and the only clock is the rest between sets. `reps` is a display string rendered verbatim, never parsed; the prescription stays the coach's words.

**What it reuses.** The 3-tab shell, the setup-then-build pattern from the activation matrix, and the entire dark theme: `members-fuerza.css` `@import`s `members-activacion.css` rather than copying its token block. *Two copies of a token block is how one dark theme becomes two slightly different dark themes.*

**Its UI chrome was built in three languages on day one** (`site/_data/strengthUi.json`), for a page that shipped only in Spanish. Directly because of §29 earlier the same day: the activation engine was written Spanish-only and quietly shipped two translated pages wrapped in Spanish buttons. **Writing three objects up front costs minutes; retrofitting an engine costs a session and ships wrong pages in between.**

***The bug, and why it matters more than the fix:*** `finish()` advances the exercise index past the end and then calls `updateUI()`, which opened with `const e = ex[idx]; if (!e) return;`. So the done overlay was never rendered — **the routine completed internally while the screen froze on the last set, with the button dead because the phase was already `done`.** The tool looked perfect for 28 of 29 sets. *It was found by a check that walked a full routine to completion, tapping through every set of all four variants; nothing that stopped earlier could have seen it.* Fixed by moving everything phase-dependent above the guard. **Worth generalising: for any stateful tool, the check has to reach the terminal state, because the terminal state is the one path no casual click-through visits.**

*Update, same day — EN and PT shipped.* `/members/en/knees/` and `/members/pt/joelhos/`, cards on both homes. **Twelve routines walked end to end** (4 variants × 3 languages), tapping through every set to the done screen. *Because the engine chrome was already three-language, this was page copy only — no engine work, no retrofit, no window where a translated page renders Spanish buttons.* That is the whole return on the decision taken a few hours earlier in §30, and it arrived the same day.

**Content:** three additions Iván chose (isometric wall sit — the only exercise here that helps someone hurting today; single-leg balance; gym adductor work), two he did not take (eccentric soleus, knee-flexion hamstring) recorded as decisions in the home doc so they are not re-raised, plus a **pain rule** (≤3/10, same or better next day) and a **red-flag boundary** — swelling, locking, giving way, night pain, post-trauma onset — that the source doc did not have. That boundary is the only warm-coloured element in the members area, deliberately: it is the one place a tool says "this might not be for you".

## 31. `/members/aquiles/` — the first artifact whose branch is clinical (August 13, 2026)

Home doc: `achilles-brief.md`. Second tool on the strength engine, built the same evening as the first; the engine needed **no changes at all**, which is the point of §30.

**Four axes → 8 routines plus a gated block**, versus the knee tool's two axes. The engine did not care: routines are lists of ids in a page-level library, and the phase array is assembled per answer. *A tool with four questions costs the same as a tool with two once the library pattern is in place.*

***The axis that matters is not a preference, it is a contraindication.*** Achilles tendinopathy is two conditions. Mid-portion responds to heel drops off a step; **insertional is compressed by dorsiflexion past neutral**, which is precisely what a heel drop off a step does at the bottom of every rep — and what a two-minute straight-leg calf stretch does deliberately. The source doc prescribed both without distinguishing. Following it, an athlete with insertional pain does about 45 compressive reps a session and concludes rehab does not work for them. **Insertional now gets flat-ground isometrics and eccentrics stopping at the floor, range-capped machine work, and no end-range calf stretch at all** — replaced by non-end-range ankle mobility whose cue explains why the stretch they expected is missing.

**The verification reflects that.** Alongside the usual structural checks, the run asserts that no insertional routine contains step work or a wall stretch, and that no mid-portion routine loses them. *That assertion is worth more than every layout check on the page:* a misplaced element is ugly, a compressive rep prescribed to an insertional tendon is harm, and both look identical to a screenshot. 16 combinations checked, 4 walked to the done screen.

**Placement decision worth keeping:** the gated jump block sits **before** the heavy strength work, not after. Energy-storage work belongs on fresh legs; plyometrics at the end of a 30-set session is how a tendon that was ready gets re-irritated on the last set.

*Update, same evening — EN and PT shipped.* `/members/en/achilles/` and `/members/pt/aquiles/`, cards on both homes. **48 combinations checked (16 × 3), 8 walked to the done screen.** ***The clinical assertion runs per language, which is the point:*** a translation pass that quietly reintroduced a calf stretch to the insertional path would read perfectly and be wrong, and no reviewer comparing three files side by side would catch it. **The check has to encode the contraindication, not the layout.**

**A test bug worth recording, because it produced a false pass.** The first verification run reported 16/16 green while never once selecting the jump block. `data-val="yes"` exists in two different option groups (roller and stage), so an unscoped `[data-val="yes"]` selector always clicked the first one. **The page was correct — its handlers are scoped per group; the check was not, and it reported success for a state it never reached.** Selectors in these runs are now scoped by `[data-axis]`. *A check that cannot reach a state cannot verify it, and will happily say it did.*

## 32. The All-Access pages stopped being hand-written (August 13, 2026)

**The failure being fixed.** All three All-Access sales pages described the members area as *"flexibility, kettlebell, fixing lower back pain, and more"*. Kettlebell was deleted that morning; the other two had never existed. The same pages sold the retired training-zones guide as a headline inclusion and named **none** of the eight real entries. *The pages were not neglected — they were hand-written, in three languages, so every library change silently invalidated three files that nobody was looking at.*

**The fix is structural, not editorial.** `site/_data/library.json` now holds the customer-facing list (live entries + a "what's coming" roadmap) in ES/EN/PT, and `partials/library-showcase.njk` renders it into all three pages. ***Shipping a tool is one edit to one file; every sales page updates.*** The technical inventory stays in `triaperformance-business-overview.md` — that file owns URLs, engines and retirements, this one owns how the same list is worded to a buyer. **Two files, two audiences, one edit each.**

**The "what's coming" block is deliberately not cards.** Cards would give unbuilt things the same visual weight as built ones, which is a milder version of the exact mistake being corrected. It renders as a dashed-border list that reads as a roadmap the coach is working through: race pacing, nutrition and carb loading, lower back, shoulder, more mental prep, pace converter.

***The emails were checked and deliberately barely changed, and the reason is the lesson.*** The n8n welcome emails never decayed, because they never named a tool — they say "the calculators and guides, and I add new tools every week", which stays true whatever ships. **The surface that named specifics rotted; the surface that named categories did not.** So the standing split is now: *emails name categories plus one durable starting point (the zones calculator, since everything else depends on zones being right); sales pages name everything and are generated.* The only email change is adding that first step, since a subscriber previously received a password and a link with nothing telling them what to do first. Paste file: `automation/members-library-email-paste.txt`.

## 33. Gemini model migration — Hermes and the research agent to `gemini-3.7-flash`, the writer deliberately left behind (August 17, 2026)

Triggered by Iván reading Google's `gemini-3.7-flash` announcement (Aug 13, 2026) and asking whether the models here should move. **Two of the four consumers moved, two did not, and the split is the point of this entry.**

### What was running, and what it cost

| Consumer | Model before | Model after | Price in/out per 1M |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hermes (VPS agent) | `gemini-3.5-flash` | **`gemini-3.7-flash`** | $1.50/$9.00 → $0.75/$3.75 |
| Research agent (`CONTENT_MODEL`) | `gemini-3.5-flash` | **`gemini-3.7-flash`** | $1.50/$9.00 → $0.75/$3.75 |
| Writer + translator (`WRITER_MODEL`) | `gemini-3.1-pro-preview` | *unchanged* | $2.00/$12.00 |
| n8n athlete-intake briefing | `gemini-3.1-pro-preview` | *unchanged* | $2.00/$12.00 |

`gemini-3.7-flash` is **GA, not `-preview`**: 1M context, 64k max output, tunable thinking levels. Introductory pricing runs to Dec 31, 2026, then $1.50/$7.50 — still below 3.5-flash on output and level on input, so the move does not become a regression at the price step.

### The finding that made this more than a version bump

**Google shipped 3.6 and 3.7 on the *Flash* line and left Pro at 3.1. There is no 3.7 Pro.** Google's own migration guidance names `gemini-3.7-flash` the recommended target for `gemini-3.5-flash`, `gemini-3-flash-preview` **and `gemini-3.1-pro-preview`** — all three models in the table above, in a single line.

*Consequence worth stating plainly, because it invalidates a rule this repo has been applying since July:* **"pro tier beats flash tier" was a claim about a model lineup that no longer exists in that shape.** Lesson 7 (flash narrating success while producing broken code) was a finding about `gemini-3.5-flash` on sustained agentic work — it was never a law about the word "flash", and 3.7 is the release that specifically targets that failure mode (DeepSWE v1.1 65.3% vs 49.0% on 3.6-flash; FrontierCode 1.1 43.6% vs 34.4%).

This also dissolves the constraint closed in `open-loops-archive.md` on Aug 10 — *"`gemini-3.1-pro-preview` is the only Pro-tier model available at Iván's account level, so there is no pinned release to move to."* That was true and is now beside the point: the pinned GA release exists, it is just not labelled Pro.

### Why the writer did NOT move

**Every benchmark Google published for 3.7 is coding, agentic and multimodal reasoning. None of it measures long-form Spanish prose in a specific brand voice, which is the writer's only job.** Moving it on the strength of a coding benchmark would be substituting a proxy for the thing being claimed — the same error shape as `check-plan-links.py` testing liveness and being read as testing correctness (§17 / `open-loops.md`).

**Cost is not an argument here either, and pretending otherwise would be dishonest about the stakes:** at ~US$0.08 per article and ~US$0.20 per ES→EN→PT set, the entire annual saving from moving the writer is smaller than one month of the VPS.

So it is settled by comparison instead: draft the same idea on both models and let Iván read the Spanish. **The review gate is what makes this cheap** — a bad draft costs a rejection, not a published article. Instructions are in the `writer_agent.py` docstring.

### The trap found on the way — and it is the recurring one

`WRITER_MODEL=gemini-3.1-pro-preview` is set in `~/.hermes/.env`, **on lines 15 and 16, twice**. Same value today, so it is silent. Two things follow:

1. **Editing the built-in default in `writer_agent.py` would have changed nothing.** Precedence is shell env → `~/.hermes/.env` / `~/.analytics/.env` → built-in default. *This is the fourth appearance of the pattern §18 and §13 exist for: **the repo copy is not automatically the thing the runtime reads.*** The agent's own `[writer] model: X (from Y)` line was written to expose exactly this and does its job — it is the reason the duplicate was findable at all.
2. **A duplicated env line is a live last-writer-wins bug** waiting for the day the two values differ. Whichever the parser wins with, nothing warns.

**Where Hermes's model actually lives — recorded here because it was documented nowhere.** `~/.hermes/config.yaml`, the **first three lines of the file**:

```yaml
model:
  default: gemini-3.5-flash      # → gemini-3.7-flash, August 17, 2026
  provider: gemini
```

*One key, one edit.* The only other `model:` keys in that file are at lines 104 and 106 (`base` and `whisper-1`) and belong to transcription — **they are not the agent's model and must not be changed**. Change procedure that worked: back up the file (it exists in no repo, so that copy is the only one), edit, validate with `python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open('/root/.hermes/config.yaml'))"` **before** restarting — a malformed YAML takes both containers down on restart and the file is the only thing that could bring them back — then `docker restart hermes-gateway hermes-dashboard`. *Note `~/.analytics/venv` has no `pyyaml`; use the system `python3`, or the container's own interpreter via `docker exec hermes-gateway python3 -c "import yaml; print(yaml.safe_load(open('/opt/data/config.yaml'))['model'])"`, which validates the syntax and prints the parsed value in one call.*

**`config.yaml` is worse and is not fixed here.** Hermes's model is set in `~/.hermes/config.yaml`, which lives only on the VPS and is in no repo — so Hermes's model change cannot be made by editing a tracked file at all. It is a dashboard edit, and it is a fifth instance of box-only configuration. *Not folded into this change deliberately: `config.yaml` sits next to `.env` in a directory whose other contents are secrets, so getting it under version control is its own scoped decision, not something to do in passing.* Logged in `open-loops.md`.

### Verification

The model swap ships **unverified against the live API** and must not be deployed before `python3 research_agent.py --test-models` confirms `gemini-3.7-flash` actually answers on this key. That flag exists precisely because *the listing endpoint reports models the key can SEE, not ones it can USE* — `gemini-2.5-pro` listed cleanly in July and then returned "no longer available to new users" on the first real call.

***Run, same day. `gemini-3.7-flash` WORKS (out=65536).*** 22 text models called, 14 usable. **The July failure is still live on this key** — `gemini-2.5-pro`, `gemini-2.5-flash` and `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` all returned "no longer available to new users," which is why this is a gate and not a formality. **`gemini-3.1-pro-preview` is confirmed from the box as the only usable pro-tier model**, so §33's central claim is now reproduced from the API rather than read off Google's blog. Also usable and previously unrecorded: `gemini-3.6-flash`, `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`, `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`, `gemma-4-31b-it`, `gemma-4-26b-a4b-it`. The `-latest` aliases (`gemini-flash-latest`, `gemini-pro-latest`) answer but are correctly excluded by the version filter — a rolling alias is the thing §3's pinned-release rule exists to avoid.

### The helper was recommending the wrong model, in the same output that proved it wrong

`--test-models` closed with `Suggested: echo 'WRITER_MODEL=gemini-3.1-pro-preview'` while listing `gemini-3.7-flash` four lines below under "worth comparing against." **It hardcoded *prefer pro over flash* and then took the newest within that bucket** — so on this key it recommended the older model with more confidence than the newer one, and named as "suggested" the exact model Google's migration guide says to migrate away from.

***The correction is not a better ranking, it is refusing to rank.*** The block now prints both tiers in version order, names the highest version number usable across both, and states in its own output that this is a version number and **not** a recommendation, with the two `--dry-run` commands to settle it by reading. *A helper that cannot evaluate Spanish prose should not be the component that picks the prose model* — and the old wording was confident enough that a future session would have read it as an answer rather than as a default.

**This is the same failure shape as the two already recorded in this repo**: `check-plan-links.py` testing liveness and being read as testing correctness, and `audit-runtime-paths.sh` reporting `outside: 0` regardless of what it found. *In all three, a tool answered a narrower question than the one its output appeared to answer, and the gap was invisible from reading it.* It surfaced here only because its recommendation contradicted a conclusion reached independently the same hour.

*It also now prints the `grep` for `WRITER_MODEL`/`CONTENT_MODEL` across both `.env` files, since its own `echo ... >>` suggestion is how the duplicate line got there.*

**Both `.env` items closed the same day:** duplicate removed, one `WRITER_MODEL=gemini-3.1-pro-preview` on line 15, verified by re-running the grep. `CONTENT_MODEL` is set in neither file, so the repo default is live and the research agent will pick up `gemini-3.7-flash` on its next Monday 06:30 run.

### Corrected in the same pass

**§3's billing line and `infrastructure.html` §3 both still said "auto-reload deliberately disabled".** Iván turned auto-reload on (a $7 floor, $30 top-up) on **August 10, 2026**; that fact was recorded in `open-loops-archive.md` and in neither of the two documents that describe the billing setup. Seven days, two stale copies, one owner. *Not harmless staleness: the retired sentence described a deliberate fail-closed design, so a silent content-engine outage on a $0 balance would have read as expected behaviour rather than as a bug.*

### Addendum, August 30, 2026 — per-caller usage accounting, and the finding that the weekly spike is not where it looked

*Raised by Iván from the AI Studio usage chart: visible daily usage plus one or two spikes a week, and the Studio UI charts usage and billing without a CSV export.*

**There is no Google AI Studio MCP** (connector registry checked, August 30, 2026) — and it would not have answered the question anyway. **Five things on this box call Gemini on ONE shared API key**: the research agent, the writer, the translator, Hermes, and the n8n intake briefing. Google attributes spend to the *key*, so the dashboard can say which model spent what and can never say which consumer did. ***The attribution gap is structural, not an export-format problem.***

**What the existing logs already contained.** `call_model()` was computing `usageMetadata` and printing it to `~/.hermes/logs/content-*.log`, which `run-agent.sh` trims at 4000 lines — the numbers existed and then expired. Read before they did:

| caller | cadence | observed |
|---|---|---|
| research | **Mondays only** | **1 call, ~25–30k tokens** — the *smallest* consumer in the engine |
| write | daily, approval-gated | 1–3 calls, ~8–33k tokens; **no run at all on many days** |
| translate | daily while a queue exists | **4 calls, ~40–52k tokens — the engine's largest line** |

***The obvious hypothesis was wrong twice.*** The research agent is the only weekly job in the content engine and it is the cheapest thing on the list. The engine's heaviest observed day is **Saturday August 22 (~61k tokens)**, not a Monday, and it is driven by **translate**. *`TRANSLATE_LIMIT=2` is counted in articles and each article costs two calls (EN + PT), so the daily ceiling is four `gemini-3.1-pro-preview` calls — and on every one of them **thinking tokens exceeded output tokens**, so billed output runs ~2.7× what the "out" figure suggests.* **The whole content engine's observed range is ~8k–61k tokens/day.**

**What that leaves — and neither goes through `call_model`, so neither is in the new table:**
1. **The Monday 03:00 Hermes coaching check-in (§14)** — agent-mode, one drafted message per active athlete. **The only other weekly LLM job on the box, and the only consumer that scales with athlete count.**
2. **Iván's own Hermes/Telegram sessions** — unbounded, agentic, and they read repo documents into context. *A single question that makes Hermes read `training_plans_inventory.csv` or a 90 KB doc costs more prompt tokens than a day of the content engine.*

***The open question is therefore not "which agent spiked" but "is the content engine even a material share of this key".*** One number settles it: total tokens for Aug 22 off the Studio chart, against ~61k. Logged in `open-loops.md`.

**Built this session, and applied and verified against the real box the same day (August 30, 2026).** *A one-word probe (`CONTENT_CALLER=manual-test`) wrote one correct row and both views returned it.* **The probe's own numbers are the argument for storing thinking separately: 7 prompt / 1 output / 108 thinking / 116 total, 13.5s — for the word "ok".** *An in/out-only table would have logged 8 tokens against 109 billed at the output rate.* **This also raises the Hermes estimate**: same model, but agentic and multi-turn, so every tool-call round trip carries thinking overhead that a turn count cannot show.

*Found while applying it, and unrelated to Gemini — logged separately in `open-loops.md`:* **`pending_drafts` and `approved_unpublished` were frozen at their pre-`topic` column list.** *Both are defined `SELECT p.*, ...`; a view freezes its `*` expansion at creation time; `content_pieces` gained `topic` in the Aug 25 rollout; and `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` cannot rename a column, so it* ***refused rather than updated*** *— silently, since nobody re-runs a schema file on a normal deploy.* `admin_service/app.py` reads both with `SELECT *`, so the review UI could not see a piece's topic for the whole window the Aug 30 grey-rectangle bug was live. Dropped and recreated; `topic` confirmed present. ***Standing lesson: `SELECT *` inside a view is a schema snapshot, not a wildcard.***

- **`model_usage` in `automation/content-engine/schema.sql`** — one row per Gemini call: `caller`, `model`, `api_version`, prompt / output / **thinking** tokens stored separately, `total_tokens`, `duration_ms`, `ok`, `http_status`, `finish_reason`. Plus `model_usage_by_day` (which days are spikes, with a weekday column) and `model_usage_daily` (who drove them).
  *Thinking is a separate column because on 3.x models `in + out ≠ total` and the gap is billed at the output rate — a table storing only in/out understates a pro call by roughly half.* **No price column**, deliberately: rates change, and a stored rate is a second copy of a figure this repo would then have to keep in sync. **`caller` is free text, not an enum**, so Hermes and the n8n briefing can be inserted later without a migration.
- **`call_model()` in `research_agent.py`** writes one row per call, on both the success and the total-failure path. *Failures are recorded at zero tokens because a burst of them is a real event and a table holding only successes cannot show one.*
- **`run-agent.sh` exports `CONTENT_CALLER="$AGENT"`** — `write` and `translate` are the same script with different flags, so argv cannot separate them and per-caller attribution would have collapsed them into one line.

**The one design constraint worth carrying forward: instrumentation must not be able to kill the thing it measures.** `log_usage()` swallows `(Exception, SystemExit)` — **`SystemExit` explicitly, and that is not paranoia**: `connect()` calls `sys.exit()` when no Postgres password is present, and `SystemExit` does not inherit from `Exception`. Catching only `Exception` would have meant a missing `PG_PASSWORD` silently killing the writer mid-article. *Verified by running `log_usage()` in an environment with no database at all: it printed `[usage] not recorded: ...` and returned normally.*

**Relevant to why this matters at all: auto-reload is ON** ($7 floor, $30 top-up, since August 10, 2026 — see §33's "Corrected in the same pass" above). **The system no longer fails closed on a spend spike**, which is the design the original billing note described and which stopped being true twenty days ago.

---

## 34. Cyclist core — rounds without teaching the engine about rounds (August 18, 2026)

Home doc: `cyclist-core-brief.md`. Third tool on the strength engine, shipped ES/EN/PT the same day. Engine unchanged again.

**The one structural idea worth reusing.** This is a *circuit*: N rounds of the same six exercises, which is not what `strength-tool.js` models — it does sets per exercise. Rather than add a rounds concept, **each round is rendered as a phase** ("Vuelta 2 de 3" / "Round 2 of 3" / "Volta 2 de 3"). The engine already walks phases in order and prints the phase name in the header, so the athlete sees which round they are in for free. ***A second concept doing the job of one that already exists is how engines get heavy; the cheap move was noticing the shapes already matched.***

Rest rides on the exercises rather than on the routine: 15s carried by exercises 1–5 of a round, 75s by the sixth, because the engine rests after an exercise's last set. No special case anywhere.

**Content review before building, which is the part that mattered.** Four changes to Iván's source doc — three his picks, one forced by the bodyweight-only call. The one worth recording here: **hollow body hold was swapped out.** It is loaded trunk flexion with active hip flexors, which is the posture a cyclist already holds for hours — so it rehearses the fault it is meant to offset. A long-lever plank gives the same anti-extension isometric without that. *Also deliberately absent: a breathing/bracing entry, offered and declined. That is the actual "deep core" connection work, and the brief records the decision so it is not later re-raised as an oversight.*

**Verification:** six variants (2 round-counts × 3 languages) walked to the done screen — 24 and 30 sets, round labels correct per language, no chrome leaks in EN or PT, no overflow at 390px.

**The library workflow also proved out.** First tool shipped since `site/_data/library.json` existed (§32): one object added there, and **all three All-Access sales pages picked it up with no page edits** — nine entries each.

## 35. The mobile nav drawer, rebuilt (August 26, 2026)

Raised by Iván from a screenshot — *"I don't like how it looks"* — on the public nav at a
sub-860px width. The complaint was cosmetic; two of the four causes were not.

**Measured before touching anything** (headless Chromium, 390×844 and 768×1024, drawer open):
the panel's content was **1368px tall inside an 844px viewport**. Both dropdowns were
permanently expanded — 8 children under *Planes*, 5 under *Recursos* — every row was 17px
with a 20px bottom margin, and the two group headings were typographically identical to
their own children. *Contacto* and the language switcher were both below the fold, in a
drawer nothing signalled was scrollable.

**Four causes, in ascending order of how much they were actually about CSS taste:**

1. **No hierarchy and no collapse.** Fixed by making the dropdown parents accordions on
   mobile only, collapsed by default, with a chevron affordance and a left rail on the
   children. Nothing is lost by not navigating on tap: `Planes` and its own first child
   `Ver todos los planes` are the same URL, and `Recursos` is `menuOnly` — it has no page
   at all. Desktop hover behaviour is untouched. Collapsed, the whole menu now fits in a
   390px viewport with room to spare; *Planes* expanded still fits without scrolling.
2. **The language switcher rendered as a vertical column with orphan separator dots.**
   `.lang-switch` is a `<span>` of links, and the drawer's own
   `header nav a { display: block; width: 100% }` turned each into a full-width row —
   producing ES / · / EN / · / PT down the page, one stranded `·` per line. It needed three
   explicit overrides to undo the block treatment, not a new component.
3. **`header nav` was never `display: flex`.** It has declared `flex-direction: column` and
   `align-items: flex-start` for as long as the drawer has existed, on an element that is a
   plain block. Both were inert. The symptom was invisible while every child was full-width
   and stacked anyway — block layout produced the same picture — and only surfaced when
   `margin-top: auto` was used to floor the utility row and did nothing. **A declaration
   that has no effect looks exactly like a declaration that is working, when the default
   happens to agree with it.** Same shape as the §23 lesson: source containing a rule is
   not evidence the rule applies.
4. **The overlay could never dim the header row.** `.nav-overlay` was a sibling of
   `.site-nav-sticky` at `z-index: 90`, against the sticky bar's 100 — so the logo strip
   stayed bright white while the rest of the page went to 45% ink, which is what made the
   open drawer read as "floating on top of a broken page". Raising the overlay is not the
   fix: **`position: sticky` always creates a stacking context regardless of `z-index`**, so
   the drawer (a descendant, `z-index: 95`) can never be escaped by an outside sibling, and
   anything raised above the header would also cover the drawer. The fix is a markup move —
   the overlay now lives *inside* `.site-nav-sticky`, where 90 puts it under the drawer and
   over the header's own non-positioned contents. One line moved; no z-index changed.

**Also added, because the drawer had no way out.** The hamburger is a non-positioned child
of the same stacking context, so an open drawer paints over it: the toggle could open the
menu and never close it, and the only exit was the overlay strip beside the panel — 70px
wide at the old 320px width on a 390px phone. The drawer now carries its own sticky close
button (`closeLabel` added to all six `nav.json` menus in three languages), plus Escape,
plus a `body.nav-open { overflow: hidden }` scroll lock so the page stops sliding around
underneath it. Panel width went to `min(400px, 92vw)`.

**One duplicate retired.** `members.css` carried its own drawer — 260px wide at a 640px
breakpoint — overriding `site.css`'s version on members content pages only. Deleted; members
pages inherit the one drawer now. *This is the hygiene rule applied to CSS: one component,
one owner. The copy was three properties different and nobody had noticed, which is exactly
how far a second copy drifts before anyone looks at it.*

**Verified in a real headless browser, not by reading the stylesheet** (per §23): desktop
1280px unchanged — toggle hidden, `nav` still `static`/`block`, chevrons and close button
and drawer utility all `display: none`, hover dropdown still opens, `Planes` still hrefs to
`/planes/`. Mobile 390px — scroll lock on, `elementFromPoint` over the logo returns the
overlay, accordion opens without closing the drawer, close button restores scroll, Escape
closes, tapping a group parent does not navigate. Members content pages checked separately
after the `members.css` deletion.

**Files:** `site/_includes/partials/nav.njk`, `site/assets/css/site.css`,
`site/assets/css/members.css`, `site/_data/nav.json`.

### §35 addendum — the hover rule that was never scoped (August 31, 2026)

Iván, on an iPhone: *"if I click Planes, the sub menu opens, if I click it again it does not
close. Same with Coaching."* And separately, in a narrowed Chrome window: hovering a group
opened it, which pushed the item below down and out from under the cursor — which closed the
group, which pulled the item back up. Unreachable without approaching sideways.

**Two reports, one line of CSS.** `.nav-dropdown:hover .nav-dropdown-menu { display: block }`
was written for the desktop dropdown and never scoped to it.

- **On iOS**, a tap applies a *sticky* `:hover` and leaves it there. The first tap ran the
  accordion handler (`.open` added, submenu shows) and also latched hover. The second tap
  removed `.open` — correctly; `aria-expanded` went to `false` — and nothing moved, because the
  latched hover rule at specificity **(0,3,0)** outranked the drawer's
  `header nav .nav-dropdown-menu { display: none }` at **(0,1,2)**. ***The handler was never
  broken. It was outvoted.***
- **In a narrow desktop window**, where hover is real, the same rule turned the accordion into a
  hover menu that reflows the list under the pointer.

**The fix is a gate, not a stronger selector.** Hover-open now lives in
`@media (min-width: 861px) and (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`. Both halves are load-bearing:
`(hover: hover)` keeps it off touch screens, `min-width: 861px` keeps it off the drawer on a narrow
mouse-driven window. Winning the specificity fight instead would have left the second symptom
entirely untouched — which is the argument for reading both reports as one bug before writing code.

**The gap that gate opens, and how it is covered.** A touch screen *wider* than 861px — an iPad in
landscape — has no hover, and under a width-only JS check would have had no tap either; `Recursos`
is `menuOnly`, so it would have become a dead label with no route to the blog. The JS predicate
therefore mirrors the CSS gate rather than checking width:
`innerWidth <= 860 || !matchMedia('(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)').matches`. ***When a
behaviour is split between CSS and JS, the two conditions have to be the same condition*** — the
original bug was exactly a handler that stopped at 861px and a rule that did not. Added alongside:
one group open at a time, outside-tap-to-close for the wide-touch case, and open groups reset when
the drawer closes.

**Why Aug 26 missed it.** That session verified this component in a real headless browser, across
five viewports, with nineteen assertions — *and every one of them was a mouse check.* Touch was
never emulated, so the single interaction mode the bug lived in was the one mode never exercised.
***A component verified in one input model is not a verified component.*** The suite now runs
iPhone 13, narrow-mouse 700px, desktop 1280px and iPad-landscape, the last covering a case neither
report mentioned.

**A failing test was wrong, and that is the second finding.** The re-run reported *"1st tap on
Coaching navigates — FAIL"*. The code was right: `nav.json` had changed underneath it on Aug 27
(§36 gave Coaching a dropdown with the new `/sobre-ivan/` pages), so Coaching was an accordion
correctly refusing to navigate, and the assertion described a nav that no longer existed. *Same
shape as the Aug 12 pattern in a new place: the note was not the artefact — and a stale assertion
fails identically to a regression.*

**The invariant that fell out of it**, now recorded in `nav.json`: **a dropdown parent with a `url`
of its own must repeat that url as one of its children.** On mobile the parent row is a toggle and
does not navigate, so a parent url no child repeats is unreachable from a phone. All three
languages satisfy it today — Planes via *Ver todos los planes*, Coaching via *Coaching 1:1*, and
Recursos is `menuOnly`, so it has no url to strand. **Check it whenever a nav item gains children.**

**Files:** `site/_includes/partials/nav.njk`, `site/assets/css/site.css`, `site/_data/nav.json`.

## Open items — retired as a section (August 8, 2026)

**This section no longer exists. Every open item across the project lives in `open-loops.md`, and closed ones in `open-loops-archive.md`. Do not start a second list here.**

It was created on July 29, 2026 as "the technical detail behind those items" and kept for ten days on that reasoning. In practice it became a parallel list that drifted: by August 8 it still described the 2,073-contact scope decision as open (decided July 29 — migrate all) and still said CoachMatch was "running side-by-side with the old HubSpot flow, not cut over", directly contradicting the sunset statement in five other docs. Two of its items had also been closed for over a week while still reading as open here.

The five items that were genuinely open and lived *only* here were promoted into `open-loops.md` on August 8: the unverified WhatsApp watchdog batch run, the missing bulk audit of leads hit by the July 31 bugs, the `plan-tracker-bigquery` Python 3.9 → 3.12 migration, the un-started MCP integrations (Google Workspace, Twenty community server), and threading Hermes into the live website workflow.

**The rule this cost us, now standing in the project instructions: one home per initiative, and one home per list.** A doc that finishes its job gets retired, not maintained.

*Technical detail behind any open item still belongs in this document — in the numbered section that owns that system (§8 n8n, §12–13 members area, §17 storefront, §18 script/config migration, §20 content engine), as a dated inline note. Just not as a list.*

*One permanently-closed finding kept here because it is a hard limitation rather than a task, and would otherwise be rediscovered: the `plan-tracker-bigquery` pixel's `referrer` column is always `https://www.trainingpeaks.com/` regardless of real traffic source. The pixel is a static `<img src="...?plan_id=X&price=Y">` tag hand-pasted into each plan's TrainingPeaks HTML description — not a JS snippet, so it has no access to the page's URL, query string or referrer at authoring time. The HTTP Referer it sees is always the page containing it. Not fixable via Caddy/Referrer-Policy, not fixable via UTM params, not a bug in the function. Real visitor-source attribution lives entirely in GA4 on triaperformance.com; this pixel's only durable signal is plan-view volume by `plan_id`.*

## Appendix: key commands reference

```bash
# Install Docker
curl -fsSL https://get.docker.com | sh

# Deploy Hermes Agent — interactive setup wizard
mkdir -p ~/.hermes && cd ~/.hermes
docker run -it --rm -v ~/.hermes:/opt/data nousresearch/hermes-agent setup

# Run a one-off interactive chat session (for testing)
docker run -it --rm -v ~/.hermes:/opt/data nousresearch/hermes-agent

# Reconfigure a single setting without redoing the whole wizard
docker run -it --rm -v ~/.hermes:/opt/data nousresearch/hermes-agent setup terminal
docker run -it --rm -v ~/.hermes:/opt/data nousresearch/hermes-agent gateway setup
docker run -it --rm -v ~/.hermes:/opt/data nousresearch/hermes-agent config set <key> <value>

# Start the Telegram gateway as a persistent background service
docker run -d --name hermes-gateway --restart unless-stopped \
  -v ~/.hermes:/opt/data nousresearch/hermes-agent gateway run

# Start the web dashboard as a persistent background service
docker run -d --name hermes-dashboard --restart unless-stopped \
  -v ~/.hermes:/opt/data -p 9119:9119 \
  nousresearch/hermes-agent dashboard --host 0.0.0.0 --port 9119 --no-open

# Tailscale setup
curl -fsSL https://tailscale.com/install.sh | sh
tailscale up          # prints a browser URL to authorize this device
tailscale ip -4        # shows this device's private tailnet IP

# Diagnostics
docker ps -a                       # container status
docker logs <container-name>       # container logs
ufw status verbose                 # firewall status

# Firewall — allow SSH + Tailscale only, deny everything else by default
ufw allow OpenSSH
ufw allow in on tailscale0
ufw enable

# Members-area — manually deactivate a subscriber token (e.g. cleaning up test data,
# or revoking access outside the normal churn-email flow)
docker exec -it analytics-postgres psql -U analytics -d members -c \
  "UPDATE subscriber_tokens SET active = FALSE, revoked_at = now() WHERE email = 'someone@example.com' AND active = TRUE;"

# Check a subscriber's token status
docker exec -it analytics-postgres psql -U analytics -d members -c \
  "SELECT email, active, revoked_at FROM subscriber_tokens WHERE email = 'someone@example.com';"

# Business knowledge base — clone + daily auto-update
git clone https://github.com/ivanKoch/triaperformance-docs.git ~/.hermes/triaperformance-docs
(crontab -l 2>/dev/null; echo "0 6 * * * cd ~/.hermes/triaperformance-docs && git pull >> ~/.hermes/logs/kb-sync.log 2>&1") | crontab -
```

## Appendix: where credentials actually live

## 36. The authority layer — credentials, the About pages, and Person structured data (August 27, 2026)

**Raised by Iván as a question, not a build request:** *"I am a TrainingPeaks partner. They send me most of the Latin American leads. Are we leveraging that enough on the website?"* A grep of `site/` answered it: **no, and the miss was sharper than "not enough."**

**What the audit found.** TrainingPeaks appeared all over the site — `+300 planes en TrainingPeaks`, `TrainingPeaks Premium incluido` six times on the All-Access pages, three `checkout.trainingpeaks.com` links, an FAQ explaining the coach-profile connection. **Every one of those describes where the transaction happens. None of them says TrainingPeaks chose him.** The word *partner* appeared **exactly once in the entire site**: `site/referidos/index.njk` line 83, inside the blurb an **athlete** copy-pastes to a friend. *The only place the relationship did credibility work was a message Iván never sends himself.* Two supporting gaps: no About page existed at all (the nav's `Coaching` entry was a bare `/#coaching` anchor with no page behind it), and the only credential anywhere was `🏅 Coach certificado IRONMAN`, hand-written three times into one sales page.

**The research changed the premise, in Iván's favour.** TrainingPeaks' own help centre calls the credential **"Level 2 Accredited"**, never *certified*, and — the part that mattered — **explicitly licenses the badge for exactly this use**: *"You can also use these images on your website and/or emails."* The same source calls Coach Match **"a limited directory of coaches"**. ***So the sanctioned, verifiable wording is also the stronger wording:*** a named accreditation and a quotable phrase from the issuer beat an invented title that no schema, directory or model can resolve. *"Partner" is not merely riskier than the truth here — for the stated goal of being recognised by search engines and LLMs, it is weaker.*

**The decision, Iván's, and the line it draws.** *Partner* **may** appear in body prose; it **must not** appear in any `<title>`, `meta description`, or JSON-LD field. **The distinction is not cosmetic and is the reason this section exists:** prose is retractable in an afternoon, structured data is copied into search indexes and training corpora and cannot be recalled. *"If they reach out I can remove it" is true of a paragraph and false of an entity fact.* A build-time check enforces it (below).

**What shipped.**

- **`site/_data/credentials.json`** — the single home for the five marks and the athletic/coaching record, ES/EN/PT. Same pattern and same justification as `library.json` (§32). Credential names are proper marks and stay in English in all three languages; only surrounding prose is translated.
- **`partials/trust-bar.njk`** — the credential strip. Full variant under the hero on the three homepages; `compact` variant on the three All-Access pages. Styles in `site.css`, not a page stylesheet, because the homepages carry no `pageCss`.
- **Three About pages** — `/sobre-ivan/`, `/en/about/`, `/pt/sobre-ivan/`, written natively per language per `brand-guidelines.md` §8, sharing `transKey: about` so hreflang wires itself. `about.css`.
- **`partials/person-schema.njk`** — a schema.org `Person` with `hasCredential` (five `EducationalOccupationalCredential` entries with `recognizedBy`) and `knowsAbout`. **This is the actual mechanism for the "make Google and the LLMs know" goal** — prose is read, structured data is *parsed*. The site had no `Person` schema at all; `infrastructure.html` still lists structured data as open Phase 1/2 SEO work, and this closes the identity half of it.
- **The 328 plan pages** — one line added to `partials/plan-page-content.njk` puts the accreditation under every plan's buy box in all three languages. **One edit, 328 indexed pages; verified 328/328 in the build.** This is the largest indexed surface the site has and was carrying no credential at all.
- **Nav** — `Coaching` became a dropdown (`Coaching 1:1` + `Sobre Iván`) so the About page has a home that reads as part of the coaching story. The parent keeps its anchor URL, so existing behaviour is unchanged.

***The `sameAs` decision is the whole strategy in one field.*** `person-schema.njk` sets `sameAs` to `trainingpeaks.com/coach/ivankoch`, **and that URL appears nowhere as a visible link on the site.** `sameAs` resolves Iván to the TrainingPeaks profile *for a machine* — which is the authority signal — **without offering a human a route into TrainingPeaks, where the same coaching athlete routes through CoachMatch at 20% instead of Private at 3.5%.** Worth **$24.59/month per athlete** (`triaperformance-pricing-and-positioning.md`). *Borrow the authority, keep the transaction.*

**Two corrections made in passing, both pre-existing:**

1. **`🏅 Coach certificado IRONMAN` → `IRONMAN U`, three places in `planes/triatlon/index.njk`.** The old wording implied IRONMAN certified him directly, which was never true, and the programme has since concluded — TriDot ended its IRONMAN sponsorship and the content moved to the **Endurance Sports Coaching Institute (ESCI)**, live at `esci.institute`. Iván holds **both** today and states IRONMAN U disappears in 2027, so it is printed **with its year** (`IRONMAN U Certified Coach · 2025`) — accurate now, and still accurate after the programme is gone rather than silently becoming a false present-tense claim.
2. **The ES homepage had been hand-typing `45 reseñas` since Aug 14**, while `social-proof-and-reviews.md` — the owning doc — had said **46** for thirteen days.
