Now rendered from `site.reviewCount`, one copy. *This is the one-owner rule catching a stale number that was live, customer-facing, and invisible because nobody re-reads a homepage sentence.*

**Verification (build + real headless Chromium, both run this session).** Clean Eleventy build, 467 files, catalogue re-derived at **328 (ES 164 / EN 111 / PT 53)** from `data/training_plans_inventory.csv`. Scripted checks all passed: three About pages exist; each emits 3 hreflang alternates plus `x-default`; the `Person` JSON-LD parses on all three and carries five credentials; **`partner` absent from every `<title>`, `meta description` and JSON-LD block, and present in body prose on all three** — the decision above, enforced rather than remembered; 5 marks render on each homepage and each All-Access page; **328/328** plan pages carry the credential line; all three About URLs in `sitemap.xml`; no bare `certificado IRONMAN` left. A Spanish-word sweep of the EN/PT rendered chrome (the §25 check) came back clean — its only hits were inside a pre-existing HTML comment in `nav.njk`. Screenshots at 1280×900 and 390×844: **zero horizontal overflow on all four**.

> ***The session's own lesson, and it is §23's again in a new place.*** The first screenshot showed the credential strip rendering as an unstyled bulleted list, which read exactly like a CSS bug in the new component. It was not. `_site/assets/css/site.css` was **stale** — the earlier build had died on a `robots.txt` passthrough copy before refreshing the CSS, so the browser was verifying real markup against a stylesheet that predated the change. **The artefact under test was not the artefact I built.** Nothing in the source could have revealed this; only re-staging the source CSS and re-shooting did. *When a live check disagrees with the source, suspect the check first — `kb-hygiene-prompt.md` item 10, earned a second time.*

### §36 addendum, same day — badges, and two CSS bugs the source could not show

Iván supplied the licensed badge files, so the strip and the credential lists now carry marks rather than only names. **TrainingPeaks ships the Level 2 Accredited badge in two variants — a white `TRAININGPEAKS` wordmark and a black one — and the black one is the only correct choice here**, because every surface it renders on is White or Blue Wash and the white wordmark would disappear, leaving a shield with no attribution. Kept at its **native 85px** (the largest TrainingPeaks supplies) and displayed at 56px, so it is never upscaled; the Stryd badge was downscaled from a 600px original to 120px. Both live in `site/assets/images/badges/` and are referenced through the `v` cache-bust filter, which **fails loud** if a path is wrong (§17) — a useful property for an asset referenced from a data file rather than a page.

The Stryd credential resolved at the same time: the mark is **Certified Stryd Coach**, the course behind it is the **Stryd Power Coaching Course**, and both are recorded — the mark as the name, the course as the note. ***No Stryd URL went into `sameAs`.*** He is listed at `stryd.com/coaches`, but that page opens a sidebar with no per-coach URL, and `sameAs` must identify *the person*: a shared directory page identifies every coach on it. **A URL that resolves to the wrong entity is worse than no URL**, and this is the same restraint the `partner` decision rests on.

> ***Two bugs, both found in a headless screenshot, both invisible in the source — §23 for the third time in one file.***
> 1. **Badges at 40px.** The markup and CSS were correct and the result was wrong: at 40px the `TRAININGPEAKS` wordmark under the shield stopped resolving and the badge degraded into an orange-and-blue smudge. **A licensed mark that is not legible is worse than no mark** — it spends the credibility and delivers none of it. Now 56px, and the strip became a **3-column grid**, because five credentials across a wrapping four-column flex row left the fifth orphaned on its own line; 3+2 reads as deliberate.
> 2. **`grid-row: 1 / -1` on the About page's credential badge.** The intent was for the image to span the name, issuer and note. `-1` resolves against the ***explicit*** grid, and only one row was declared — so the badge spanned exactly one row, and the issuer and note auto-placed back into column 1, rendering **on top of the image**. Replaced with the flex + `.cred-text` wrapper the strip already used. *The declaration was not wrong in isolation; it was wrong about a grid that did not exist. Reading it in the stylesheet tells you nothing — only the rendered box does.*


### §36 addendum 2, same day — the credential strip rebuilt after seeing it on the real homepage

**Iván's verdict on the live page: "it looks terrible."** He was right, and the reason is worth recording because the component tested fine in isolation twice.

**What was wrong.** The strip rendered each credential as a name *plus its explanatory note*, in a three-column grid. On the real homepage — which has a full-bleed photo hero, not the flat fallback colour my local screenshots showed — that produced **a ~450px block of body copy immediately under the hero**: five long names, five sentences, in a ragged 3+2 grid where the two badged rows were indented by the badge width and the three text-only rows were not, so nothing lined up with anything. ***A trust strip is glanced at, not read.*** Every one of those sentences already exists on the About page, which is where someone who wants the explanation has chosen to go.

**What it is now.** One wrapping row of marks, vertically centred, **names only** — `item.note` is never rendered in the strip. Section padding 40px → 28px, one border instead of two, label margin 20px → 14px, names 15px → 14px, badges 56px → 40px. **Measured on the rebuilt page: 226px on desktop, 292px on mobile, zero horizontal overflow at 1440, 1100 and 390.** `short` labels were added to `credentials.json` for the strip; `name` stays the full mark and is what the About pages and the `Person` schema use.

**Two judgement calls left deliberately alone.**

- **The badge is 40px here and 56px on the About page, and that is not an inconsistency to "fix".** In the strip the badge sits directly beside its own name in text, so it only has to be recognisable as a shape and a colour. On the About page it stands alone above a note and has to carry its own wordmark, which needs the size. *The earlier 40px-everywhere version failed precisely because the About page instance had no text beside it.*
- **The fifth mark wraps to a second line and stays there.** It can be made to fit by trimming the repeated "Coach"/"Certified" out of the labels, and that was rejected: ***these are issuer-granted marks and shortening them to win a line break is the same category of error as inventing "partner"***, only smaller. The wrap is cosmetic; the wording is a claim.

> ***The process lesson, which is not the same as §23's.*** §23 was *a rule in the source is not a rule that applies*. This one is narrower and newer: **a component verified in isolation is not a component verified in place.** Both prior screenshots rendered the hero as flat `--ink` because the background image is set in CSS and my local server had no `assets/images/`, so I judged a wash block against a flat dark rectangle instead of a photograph. The strip was measured, checked for overflow, and passed — and it was still wrong, because the thing it had to sit under was not in the picture. **Stage the page's actual assets, not only the files you changed.**


### §36 addendum 3, same day — the strip became a logo band, and moved down the page

**Iván, on seeing the compact strip live: the position itself is unnatural.** He was right and it is a different objection from the previous one. Addendum 2 fixed the strip's *weight*; this fixes its *place*. **A credential block is not what a visitor wants immediately after the headline** — they want to know what is on offer. The band now sits **after the testimonials and before the final CTA**: athlete proof, then institutional proof, then the ask.

**Grouped by issuing body, not by credential.** TrainingPeaks issued two of the five, and a row showing the TrainingPeaks mark twice reads as a mistake rather than as thoroughness. Four tiles — TrainingPeaks, IRONMAN U, ESCI, Stryd — each with its logo and a caption naming the credential(s) that body granted. ***The band never restates a credential name***: `_data/credentialsBand.js` resolves item ids against `credentials.json` and joins the captions, so `items` stays the single owner of the wording and adding a credential means adding it once plus an id to a band row.

*That data file exists because of a real Nunjucks limit, not preference:* joining captions from nested loops needs array mutation, `caps.push(...)` throws (`Unable to call caps["push"]`), and emitting the separator inline produced `Level 2 Accredited Coach ·  ·  Coach Match ·` — **the inner loop cannot know whether the outer one has more to come.** The file also throws on an id that no longer exists, same fail-loud reasoning as the `v` filter in §17.

**New copy, Iván's, with one figure and one verb that matter.** *"Triaperformance es partner de TrainingPeaks. Ellos nos derivan atletas de toda Latinoamérica: ya hablamos con más de 1.500 sobre sus objetivos deportivos."* The **1,500** is stated by Iván and is a live metric, so it stands. ***The verb is load-bearing and must not drift: this is leads spoken with, not athletes coached.*** The About pages on the same site say **+100 atletas entrenados** and `data/athlete_tenure.csv` holds 98 coaching relationships since May 2024 — so "1.500 atletas entrenados" anywhere would put two numbers on one site that contradict each other. **Kept precise, the pair is an asset rather than a conflict:** 1,500 conversations producing ~100 coached athletes is evidence of referral volume *and* selectivity. Recorded with that reasoning in `credentials.json`.

**Logo handling.**

- **IRONMAN U** (4000×1000, transparent) trimmed to its bounding box and downscaled to 394×96. **ESCI** is only 252×36 and crops to 242×24 — stored **native**, because a 3× upscale produced a 71KB visibly blurry file and *soft beats blurry*. A larger PNG or an SVG from ESCI is logged as an open item.
- Wordmarks are capped at 30px tall and badges at 56px inside a shared 64px band. **Not an inconsistency:** normalising both to one raw height would leave a 200px-wide lockup optically dwarfing a circular badge beside it. Optical weight, not pixel height.
- ***The plain TrainingPeaks wordmark Iván asked for is NOT used, and that is a blocker on his side rather than a decision against it.*** The file supplied (888×356) has an **opaque dark-navy background baked in** — `alpha_min` 255, corners `#040F2F`. In a white band beside three transparent wordmarks it is a dark rectangle. **Keying the background out is not an option: that alters a trademark**, and TrainingPeaks publishes usage rules. When Iván supplies the transparent variant, TrainingPeaks can take two tiles by splitting the `tp` band row's `items`. Until then the licensed Level 2 Accredited shield carries both — arguably the better mark regardless, since it says TrainingPeaks *and* names the credential where the wordmark says only TrainingPeaks.

**Verified:** clean build, 4 logo tiles on all six pages, all four images loading at their intended sizes, no doubled caption separators, band positioned after `.pricing` on all three homepages, `partner` present in prose and absent from every title and meta description (the homepages carry no `Person` schema, so there is nothing to leak into), About pages and their 5-credential schema untouched, 328/328 plan pages, zero horizontal overflow at 1440 and 390. `trust-bar.njk` was moved to `_to_delete/` — this sandbox cannot delete inside the repo, so it needs `git add -A` to leave the tree.


### §36 addendum 4 — band captions print the full mark, and `capt` was deleted rather than rewritten

**Iván:** *"it might be repetitive with the image, but we are also looking for crawlers."* **Correct, and it is the right trade.** A logo is an image, and an image asserts nothing to a search engine or a language model — `alt` text helps, a caption naming the mark in body text helps more. Captions are now the complete marks: *TrainingPeaks Level 2 Accredited Coach · TrainingPeaks Coach Match*, *IRONMAN U Certified Coach · 2025*, *ESCI Certified Coach — Endurance Sports Coaching Institute*, *Certified Stryd Coach*.

***The per-item `capt` field was deleted, not edited.*** Once a caption is the full mark, `capt` is a second copy of `name` — and a second copy of a credential name is precisely the thing this repo's one-owner rule exists to prevent. `credentialsBand.js` now derives the caption from `name`, and the only per-item addition is `captSuffix`, carrying what is *not* part of the mark: IRONMAN U's year, ESCI's expanded organisation name. **`captSuffix` is appended for display only and never reaches the `Person` schema** — asserted in the verification pass, because a `hasCredential.name` of "ESCI Certified Coach — Endurance Sports Coaching Institute" would be a mark that no issuer grants.

> ***One instruction was deliberately not followed literally.*** Iván asked for *"ESCI - Endurance Sports Coaching Institute Certified Coach"*. That interpolates the expansion into the middle of the mark and splits **"ESCI Certified Coach"** into two non-contiguous pieces — and a contiguous mark string is exactly what an entity matcher looks for, so the literal version would have *cost* the crawler value it was asked for. Rendered mark-first with the expansion after it: same words, same length, mark intact.

> **Repo note, same session.** Twenty new blog posts, an EN `/en/referrals/` page and `/members/fuerza/` appeared in the tree mid-session (build went 467 → 489 pages, 81 → 146 passthrough files) — parallel work, not part of this branch. No collision: `nav.njk` was touched on that side, this work touched `nav.json`. Flagged to Iván because `git add -A` sweeps it all into one commit.


**Two open items go to `open-loops.md`, neither blocking:** the exact name on the Stryd certificate (printed conservatively as `Stryd — Running with Power` until confirmed), and the 2027 removal of the IRONMAN U entry.

No secret values are recorded in this document, by design. For reference, here is *where* each credential is stored:

| Credential | Location |
|---|---|
| Gemini API key | `~/.hermes/.env` on the VPS (`GOOGLE_API_KEY` or similar) |
| Telegram bot token | `~/.hermes/.env` on the VPS |
| Dashboard username/password | `~/.hermes/.env` on the VPS (`HERMES_DASHBOARD_BASIC_AUTH_*`), file permissions restricted to `600` |
| Nous Portal OAuth client ID | `config.yaml` (`dashboard.oauth.client_id`) — not a secret value, safe to store in config |
| Tailscale device auth | Managed by the Tailscale client itself, tied to the account's login |
| BigQuery service account key (`pixel-sync-vps`, read-only) | `~/.analytics/credentials/bigquery-sa.json` on the VPS, permissions `600` |
| Analytics Postgres password + BigQuery table reference | `~/.analytics/.env` on the VPS, permissions `600` |

## 37. The first Ahrefs crawl acted on — two 404s, 59 alt-less images, and a build check so neither recurs (September 1, 2026)

**Input:** five Ahrefs Site Audit exports from the Sept 1, 2026 crawl (404 pages, 4xx pages, page-has-links-to-4xx, missing alt text, meta description changes). **Three of the five were the same two problems seen from different angles, and one was not a problem at all.**

### What was actually wrong

**(a) Two broken internal links, both authored, neither ever deployed as a page.**

| Bad link | Linked from | Correct target |
|---|---|---|
| `/calculadora-de-zonas/cycling/` | `blog/metodo-noruego-ciclismo-entrenamiento-umbral.njk`, `blog/mito-ftp-estatico-ciclismo.njk` | `/calculadora-de-zonas/ciclismo/` |
| `/coaching/` | `blog/plan-entrenamiento-maraton-lesionado.njk` | `/#coaching` |

***Both are the same class of mistake and it is worth naming: a URL that was reasoned about rather than looked up.*** The first is the **English** path segment inside a **Spanish** URL — the EN tree really does serve `/en/training-zones-calculator/cycling/`, so `cycling` is a real segment on a real page, just not that one. The second is a page that has **never existed**: the nav's `Coaching` entry has always been the homepage anchor `/#coaching` (see `_data/nav.json`, which documents why anchor targets are absolute), and `/coaching/` is what a plausible site *would* have.

**(b) 59 linked images with no alt text**, all from one line — `_includes/partials/post-card.njk` rendered the blog card photo as `alt=""`. Three URLs in the report (`/blog/`, `/en/blog/`, `/pt/blog/`) because all three listings share the partial; one fix covers all of them. ***The empty alt was not a bug — it is WCAG-correct for a decorative photo inside a link that already carries text.*** Ahrefs' check is "linked image has no alt text", which an empty alt does not satisfy. Resolved with `alt="{{ post.data.headline }}"`: unique per card, already translated, no new i18n key, and no fallback needed for an `articles/<slug>.jpg` pinned to an article with no `topic`. *The accepted cost is that a screen reader hears the headline twice per card; the reasoning is recorded in the partial itself so it is not "fixed" back in six weeks.*

**(c) The meta-description export was a change report, not an issue.** One row, `/planes/triatlon/`: `certificado IRONMAN` → `certificado IRONMAN U`. **That is the §36 correction from Aug 27 arriving in a crawl five days later** — it is the fix, not a finding. *Worth knowing for future crawls: Ahrefs' "Meta description" export lists what changed since the previous crawl, so a clean deploy shows up there and reads like a defect at a glance.*

### The durable half — `tests/internal-links.test.js`

**The writer agent invents internal links and has no list of real URLs.** Giving it one is the wrong fix: the URL set is a *product of the build*, so any list handed to a prompt is stale the next time a page is added or a slug changes. The build is the only thing that knows which paths exist, so the check belongs there.

Added `tests/internal-links.test.js`, wired into `npm test` alongside the two zones suites, following the same contract as `zones-ui.test.js` — **it runs against `_site/`, so `npm run build` must have run first**, because the thing being tested is what Caddy will serve rather than what a template says. Two assertions:

1. **Every root-relative `href`/`src` resolves** to a built file, or to a directory containing `index.html`. Scope is deliberately narrow — external links belong to somebody else's server and would make this a network call; `//`, `mailto:` and `tel:` are not paths. Fragments and query strings are stripped before resolving.
2. **Every `<img>` carries a non-empty `alt`.** Missing *and* empty both fail, which is the Ahrefs rule rather than the WCAG one — a deliberate choice, since the site has no decorative images that a caption could not serve.

***The test was verified against a fixture that fails, not only against a site that passes*** — same principle as the `gbp_keywords` guard test in §9. A checker that has only ever seen green is not evidence of anything.

**This turns "a crawler finds a broken link three weeks later" into "the build fails."** Current state: 488 pages scanned, **0 broken internal targets, 0 images without alt text**.

### One local-environment note, not a repo defect

Building from the Cowork device bridge fails at the passthrough-copy step with `EPERM: unlink '_site/robots.txt'` — that shell cannot delete files, and Eleventy clears passthrough targets before recopying them. **Pages are still written correctly**; only the copy step errors. `npm run build` from Iván's own terminal is unaffected.

---

### §38 — Addendum, September 2, 2026 — strength guide translated to EN and PT

`/members/en/strength/` and `/members/pt/forca/` built as copy-only siblings of `/members/fuerza/`. Same `pageCss` (`members.css` + `members-fuerza-guia.css`), same goal-switch script, same two 12-week periodization paths, same numbers. Front matter carries only `layout`, `pageCss` and `title`; `lang`, `noindex`, `navVariant` and `footerVariant` are inherited from `site/members/members.json` and the per-language `en.json` / `pt.json`, per `artifact-publish-runbook.md` Phase 2. Cards added to `site/members/en/index.njk` and `site/members/pt/index.njk`.

Verified against the built output, not the templates: 2 analytics tags per page, `noindex, nofollow` present, 0 unconsumed `{% raw %}` tags, 0 sitemap entries, `npm test` green (12 unit + 2 internal-link checks, the latter now resolving the two new card links).

> ***The finding is not the pages, it is what the build revealed about the two inventories.*** `triaperformance-business-overview.md` §Interactive tools and this runbook's Published-artifacts table are the repo's inventories of record for the members library, and **neither listed `/members/fuerza/` at all** — it shipped in August 2026 and was added to neither. So while `site/_data/library.json` sold `strength` as live and gated in English and Portuguese, **the two documents a session would open to verify that claim did not contain the tool, and therefore could not contradict it.** *This is §36's lesson in a new place and the runbook's own August 14 correction repeating: that correction re-derived the table from the filesystem, counted eight tools, and the strength guide was already the ninth.* **An inventory is only a control if the thing it is supposed to catch would appear in it.** Both corrected the same session; the library is ten entries in three languages, two of them guides.

> *Standing check added to `kb-hygiene-prompt.md` Step 0: diff **every** key in `library.json` against `site/members/` for all three language blocks, every pass. Twice now — cyclist core on Aug 24, strength on Sep 2 — the gap was found while looking at a different key.*

### §18 addendum, September 2, 2026 — the runtime-path audit was RUN for the first time

*The script had existed since Aug 12 and never been executed; the item's own text said it closes
on a clean output, not on the script existing. It is not clean.*

**Four files run from outside the clone. Three are real, one is noise:**

| file | lines | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `/root/.hermes/deploy-website.sh` | 165 | ~~real~~ **NOT an orphan — self-updating, see the retraction below** |
| `/root/.members-auth/auth_service/app.py` | 171 | ~~real~~ **stale copy — NOT the build source** |
| `/root/.analytics/scripts/sync_pixel_data.py` | 105 | almost certainly a dead orphan |
| `/root/.hermes/lazy-packages/typing_extensions.py` | 4317 | **false positive — vendored upstream** |

> ✅ ***Both settled the same evening, and only ONE of the four was a genuine orphan.***
>
> **`members-auth` builds from the clone.** *Its compose file reads* `build: /root/.hermes/triaperformance-docs/automation/members-area/auth_service` *— so the Aug 12 §13 fix did take, and the file sitting at `/root/.members-auth/auth_service/app.py` is a stale copy that nothing builds from.* ⚠️ **That is a trap rather than a defect: it is the file a person would open, next to the compose file, and editing it changes nothing.** *Delete it.* ***And it is a false positive of a kind this script cannot avoid — it scans fixed paths and cannot know a container builds elsewhere. The `build:` line is the only thing that answers it.***
>
> **`sync_pixel_data.py` at `/root/.analytics/scripts/` is dead.** *`crontab -l | grep -c analytics/scripts` returns* **0**; *the live line runs the clone copy.* ⚠️ ***But three documents still named the orphan as the live script — including that script's own docstring — so the stale path, not the stale file, was the real hazard.*** *All three corrected.*
>
> **Final tally, after the retraction: ZERO genuine orphans. Two stale copies to delete, one vendored false positive, and one self-updating script the audit could not recognise.**

***All three real ones have identical line counts to their repo copies*** — 165, 171 and 105 —
so nothing has drifted yet. **That is the whole reason to fix it now: the cost of this problem is
zero today and unbounded on the first edit.**

> 🚨 ***RETRACTED the same evening, September 3, 2026: `deploy-website.sh` is not an orphan and never was.*** *Its last step, lines 148–163, `cmp`s itself against `automation/deploy-website.sh` and copies the repo version over itself —* **at the END, after a successful deploy**, *so a bad commit cannot brick the deploy path and the new version only takes effect on the next run.* **That is why all three line counts matched: mechanism, not luck.** ***The comment there already names the hazard this session then 'discovered' independently*** *— "bash reads scripts incrementally, so rewriting the running file mid-execution is its own class of bug" — and solves it better than the dispatcher that was written to fix it, which would have pulled and exec'd on every run and made a bad commit take effect immediately. The dispatcher is withdrawn.* ⚠️ **The audit was not wrong so much as blind: it models two shapes — inside the clone, or a dispatcher — and a self-updating script is a third.** *It now reports a file byte-identical to a same-named file in the clone as `MIRROR` rather than `OUTSIDE`, because that file does have version history and the only open question is what keeps it in sync.* ***The lesson is the one this repo keeps re-learning from the other direction: a diagnostic that over-reports costs real work. An hour went into fixing something that was already handled, and the answer was fifteen lines into the file itself.***

~~**`deploy-website.sh` is the one that matters,**~~ because it is the script that deploys the site
*and* validates and installs `/etc/caddy/Caddyfile`. The cutover note in §15 says it plainly —
"replace `~/.hermes/deploy-website.sh` with the version in `automation/`" — a manual copy, made
once, that never updates itself. Every Caddyfile change reviewed in the repo since is a change to
a file the box does not read.

⚠️ ***And the obvious fix is wrong here.*** The standing §18 practice for a plain crontab line is
to point the line into the clone. `deploy-website.sh` does `git fetch && git reset --hard` **on
the clone it would then be executing from**, and bash reads a script lazily — a reset landing
mid-run can swap the file under the interpreter and execute a splice of two versions. Rare,
silent, and awful to debug. `automation/deploy-website-dispatcher.sh` therefore pulls first and
then `exec`s a **private `mktemp` copy** that no reset can touch. *The crontab line does not
change.*

**The script was also corrected in the same pass:** vendored paths (`lazy-packages`,
`site-packages`, `dist-packages`, `node_modules`, `venv`) are now excluded. *A diagnostic that
cries wolf is the same failure as one that under-reports, arriving from the other side — and the
under-reporting version of this exact script was the defect found on Aug 12.*

**Confirmed clean and not to be re-audited:** every `CHECK` container row is a data or
third-party bind mount (`/root/.n8n`, `/root/.analytics/data`, `/root/.hermes` as `/opt/data`),
and all eleven cron lines that touch our code `cd` into the clone and `git pull` first.

---

## 39. Movilidad post-entreno — a third tool on the activation engine, and the chrome string that could not be overridden (September 3, 2026)

`/members/movilidad/` — 15 routines (5 sports × 10/20/30 min) on the activation engine. **All design, dosing and clinical reasoning lives in `mobility-brief.md`, which is the home doc.** This section records only what is *shared*, because that is the part another page can trip over.

**The engine and the matrix pattern were reused unchanged**, exactly as §29 describes: the page renders its own setup, builds `window.ACTIVATION_DATA`, then injects `activation-tool.js`, which sees an ordinary timed routine. Third tool on that engine after the activation matrix and core. **Nothing about the engine changed, again**, and the additive tier structure (10 ⊂ 20 ⊂ 30) is data, not code.

**Two shared files did change, both additively:**

1. **`partials/activation-tool.njk` — the done title now has an `id`.** It was `<div class="done-title">{{ aui.doneTitle }}</div>`, baked in at build time from `activationUi.json`. Nothing else sets it, so every existing page renders identically.

2. **`members-activacion.css` — `.setup-opts--wrap`**, a modifier for a question with more than three options. `.setup-opts` children are `flex: 1`, which is right for the two and three-option questions the activation matrix asks and produces 61–72px buttons for five.

🚨 ***The transferable finding is #1, and it is a variant of §29 arriving from a new direction.*** §29 fixed the activation engine being hardcoded Spanish by moving its chrome into `activationUi.json`, **keyed by language**. That file still has no concept of *which tool is asking* — so a mobility tool inherits `"Empezar activación →"` and `"Activación completa"`, which are correct Spanish and wrong copy. Three strings needed correcting and **each one lands in a different place**:

- `kicker` — the engine already accepts a per-routine override (`D.kicker`).
- `startRoutine` — read through `t()` at render time, so patching `window.ACTIVATION_UI` before boot works.
- `doneTitle` — **is not an engine string at all.** ***A `window.ACTIVATION_UI` override for it silently does nothing***, because the partial already wrote the text into the markup. Hence the `id`.

**A fourth tool on this engine will hit the same wall, and the general shape is this: a data file keyed on one dimension will not carry a second one, and the failure is silent on whichever strings are server-rendered rather than read at runtime.** *The check that caught it was a browser walk to the finish screen — not a data test, and not a reading of the override code, which looked correct.*

⚠️ **Not a defect, recorded so it is not re-investigated:** a local `npx @11ty/eleventy` does **not** refresh a changed file under `site/assets/` into an existing `_site/` — passthrough copy skips it, so a CSS change appears to have no effect. The `?v=` fingerprint is computed from the **source** file and was correct throughout, and `deploy-website.sh` builds into a fresh `mktemp -d`, so **production is unaffected**. *Build to a temp dir when verifying an asset change locally.*

---

## 40. The activation engine printed Spanish on four EN/PT pages for three weeks (September 4, 2026)

Found while shipping the mobility tool in English and Portuguese, by **looking at a rendered Portuguese page** — not by the build, not by `build-sanity.js`, and not by 534 data checks running against the same engine.

`activation-tool.js` built its exercise counter as:

```js
$("exNum").textContent = "Ejercicio " + (idx + 1) + " de " + exercises.length;
```

**The one string in that file that never went through `t()`.** So `/members/en/activation/`, `/members/pt/ativacao/`, `/members/en/core/` and `/members/pt/core/` have been showing English and Portuguese subscribers **"Ejercicio 3 de 14"** since August 13, 2026.

🚨 ***The instructive part is that §29 IS the pass that fixed hardcoded Spanish in this engine.*** It moved the strings that were already routed through `t()` and the ones the partial rendered — and this one was neither, so the pass explicitly about this defect walked straight past this instance of it. **A pass that fixes a class of defect does not fix the instances it cannot see, and afterwards "we did an i18n pass" reads as coverage.** *Same shape as §39's `doneTitle`, one day earlier and in the same file: the string that does not go through the mechanism is invisible to any check aimed at the mechanism.*

**Fixed** with `exerciseNum` in `activationUi.json` for all three languages — **a template (`"Exercise {n} of {total}"`) rather than two keys, because the word order is not the same in all three** — and a `t()` call in the engine with the old string as its fallback. **Seven pages verified rendering the counter in their own language**: the three mobility pages plus the four that were already wrong.

`tests/mobility-matrix.js` §9.6 now asserts the engine never hardcodes it again, that every language block carries `exerciseNum` with both placeholders, and **that all three chrome blocks have identical key sets** — a missing key renders the literal word `undefined` on a paid page, which is the failure this file has always been one typo away from.

⚠️ **Nothing checks `strength-tool.js` the same way**, and it serves four tools in three languages each. It was built with three-language chrome on day one *because* of §29, so it is more likely clean — **but that is exactly what was believed about this engine yesterday.** Logged in `open-loops.md`: one grep per engine for a quoted Spanish string outside a `t()` call.

## 41. The All-Access sales pages and the members homes both render from `library.json` (September 4, 2026)

**What changed.** `site/_data/library.json` gained four fields per live entry — `category`, `memberUrl`, `memberCta`, `memberDesc` — plus `categories`, `categoryOrder` and `categoryCounts` per language. A new partial `partials/members-library.njk` renders the members-area grid from it, and `partials/library-showcase.njk` now groups the sales grid the same way. **The three members homes stopped hand-writing their thirteen cards**, which closes the `open-loops.md` item that predicted they would drift "the next time a tool ships".

**Why two description fields and not one.** `desc` sells to a stranger on a public page; `memberDesc` orients somebody who has already paid and is choosing what to open. They were different in the hand-written originals, deliberately, and were lifted verbatim rather than merged. *Collapsing them would make the sales page read like documentation or the members page read like an ad.*

### The grouping, and the taxonomy that was rejected

Three buckets: **`train`** (Antes y después de entrenar, 5) · **`pain`** (Cuando algo duele, 3) · **`numbers`** (Entender tus números, 5). Identical membership in all three languages.

⚠️ ***A pre/post/recovery split was proposed first and mapped against the real thirteen before being rejected.*** *It produced four buckets holding exactly one card each (calculators → zones; pre → activation; post → mobility; recovery → breathing), with the two core routines belonging to none of them.* **A grid of single-card categories makes a thirteen-tool library look thinner than a flat grid does**, which inverts the goal. **Standing rule, now in the file's own comment: if a new category would hold one card, it is a tag, not a category.** *The second reason is that pre/post is a **schedule** taxonomy — it answers "when do I do this", while a reader on a sales page is asking "is there anything here for me". The buckets are problem-shaped for that reason.*

### Two traps found while building it

🚨 **`selectattr` does not work here. Nunjucks is not Jinja2.** *The first implementation filtered each bucket with `lib.live | selectattr("category", "equalto", cat) | list`. It threw no error, produced no warning, and* **rendered all thirteen cards under all three headings** *— 39 cards on a page that should carry 13.* **A build that succeeds is not a page that is right**, and this one looked correct in the template source. *Replaced with an inline `{% if item.category == cat %}` inside the loop; `categoryCounts` exists purely so the "is this bucket empty" guard needs no filter either. The obvious next attempt — building the array with `items.push()` inside a `{% for %}` — also fails, on Nunjucks' loop scoping.*

🚨 **A single source of truth protects the thing it renders and nothing else.** *`library.json` was created Aug 13, 2026 so the three All-Access pages would stop hand-listing the library. It worked: the **list** stayed correct through five tool ships. The **sentence above the list** was hand-written, said "Ocho herramientas" / "Eight" / "Oito", and was wrong by five for three weeks — Post-Workout Mobility, Swimmer's Shoulder and the strength guide all shipped after it was typed.* **The count now renders as `{{ library[lang].live | length }}` and the card names no tools at all.** *A hand-written summary sitting beside generated content is exactly as fragile as the content used to be, and harder to spot because everything around it is correct.*

### `members-home.css` had the §26 backdrop-filter bug all along

**`/members/` reported `document.scrollWidth` 780 against a 390px viewport** — the `open-loops.md` item opened Sep 3, which recorded the symptom ("exactly double") and no cause. ***The cause is §26's, verbatim: `backdrop-filter` makes an element the containing block for `position: fixed` descendants, so the closed slide-out menu parked at `right: -100%` stops being viewport-fixed and starts counting toward document width.*** *Confirmed by setting `backdrop-filter: none` in a live page and watching `scrollWidth` return to 390.*

🚨 ***The finding is not the bug, it is why §26 did not fix it.*** *That pass fixed `.site-nav-sticky` — the **subpage** nav variant — in `members-activacion.css` and the other dark subpage stylesheets. The members **home** uses `.site-nav`, a different selector in a different stylesheet, and was never in scope.* **That is exactly why the tool pages under it — `hombro`, `rodillas`, `aquiles` — all correctly reported 390 while the page above them did not**, which the Sep 3 item noticed and read as evidence the tools were innocent rather than as evidence the fix had a seam in it. ***Same shape as §40 and §29: a pass that fixes a class of defect does not fix the instances it cannot see, and afterwards the class reads as handled.***

**Fixed with §26's own fix, not a new one** — `@media (max-width: 860px) { .site-nav { backdrop-filter: none } }` — deliberately rather than the pseudo-element alternative that would have preserved the blur on mobile. *Four stylesheets now carry one identical fix at one identical breakpoint; a second technique for the same defect is how the next person concludes there are two different bugs.* **The 860 is wider than this file's own 700px drawer breakpoint on purpose:** between 700 and 860 the drawer is not in use, so dropping the blur costs nothing and keeps the number consistent. *Verified 390 / 768 / 860 / 1440, and the drawer still opens to `right: 0`.*

### Also fixed on the sales pages

- **The link inside the featured card was invisible.** `.include-card.featured` sets a `--blue` background and had no rule for `a`, so the anchor kept the site link colour — the same `--blue`. **Contrast 1:1.** Now white and underlined, *underlined because on a solid fill colour alone is not a reliable affordance.*
- **`Qué incluye` went from seven cards to four** and the grid is 2×2, scoped to `.includes-section` so the 13-card showcase below keeps three columns. *Four cards in a three-column grid leaves the last one alone on its own row, which reads as a card that failed to load.*
- **The "in three languages" claim came out of the card and the section intro**, in all three languages. *Iván's call: a Spanish-speaking athlete does not care that the tool also exists in Portuguese — it is a fact about the business, not a benefit to the reader, on a page they already reached in their own language.*

*Offer changes on those pages — the three guide cards removed, coach support added, the weekly live call retired — are positioning decisions and live in `triaperformance-pricing-and-positioning.md`, not here.*

### Same-day follow-up — catalogue counts, the plans card, and a third instance of the leaking-selector family

**`site/_data/plans.js` now returns `counts`** — `total`, `byLang`, and `byLangSport` (including a derived `runBikeTri`) — computed from the same rows the catalogue is built from. *The All-Access pages needed "my catalogue of N plans", and this repo has now been bitten by a hand-typed number three separate times: `Ocho herramientas` against a real thirteen, a 303-plan catalogue in `pricing-and-positioning.md` against a real 328, and a review count that read 45 for thirteen days (§36).* **A number a template can derive should never be a number a template contains.**

*`byLangSport` exists for a real asymmetry rather than for symmetry:* **the Portuguese product is deliberately narrower** — its page sells running, cycling and triathlon, not the catalogue — *so PT renders `runBikeTri` (45) while ES and EN render their language totals (164 / 111) plus the 328 grand total.* ⚠️ **That asymmetry is itself an unresolved product question, logged in `open-loops.md`:** the pricing doc says the subscription grants the entire catalogue and that "all plans included" is approved copy, while the PT page has always claimed three disciplines. *The copy kept the narrow claim; widening it is a product decision, not a copy fix.*

**The plans card was rewritten because the claim did not explain itself.** *"Todos los planes de entrenamiento" is true, is the largest thing in the offer, and tells a reader who does not already know TrainingPeaks' plan-assignment model nothing at all.* The card now names the delivery mechanism (`dentro de TrainingPeaks`), the size, that plans load into the reader's own calendar, that more than one can run at once, and that switching is free. **This is a patch, not the fix** — the fix is a demonstration (video and/or a screenshot walkthrough) and is logged as its own item. *Note the class of defect: the library copy was **wrong**, this copy was **true and unintelligible**, and only the first kind is catchable by an inventory check.*

🚨 ***A third instance of §41's own lesson, found the same afternoon.*** *Iván reported the credentials band's text "only uses half the area" and added that* **"this bug keeps happening"**. *The cause is `all-access.css` line 33: a **bare** `h2 { text-align: center }` written for the page's own sections, which reaches into every shared partial the page includes.* `partials/credentials-band.njk` *is left-aligned by design and renders correctly on the homepage and the About pages —* ***which do not load that stylesheet*** *— so only the All-Access copy was broken, and the rule that broke it lives in a different file from the symptom.* **Measured before fixing: every element in the band at `left=204`, the `h2` alone at `text-align: center`, over a lead capped at 65ch.** *Fixed with `.cred-band h2 { text-align: left; }` — restoring the partial's design rather than un-centring the page.*

**Put beside §26 (`.site-nav-sticky` fixed, `.site-nav` missed) and §41's members-home finding, this is one family: a selector that is correct for the author's page and wrong for everything else that shares the DOM with it.** *The generalised item — audit page-scoped stylesheets for bare element selectors — is in `open-loops.md` rather than here, because it is work, not a record.*

**Also renamed:** the `core` library entry is now **Core para el Hotel** / **Hotel Room Core** / **Core para o Hotel**, in `library.json` and in the three artifact pages' `title` and `ACTIVATION_DATA.title`. *URL unchanged, no redirect needed. The pages already carried `context: "Habitación de hotel · sin equipo"` and a travel-only `why`, so the title was the last part of the tool not saying what it was for.* **And the "Hacé la cuenta" comparison gained a members-area line in all three languages** — it had been comparing plans-and-Premium against plans-and-Premium while the library sat unmentioned in the one section of the page that does the arithmetic.

### Same-day, third pass — the homepage services section reframed, and two measure caps removed

**The heading was wrong on the facts.** *"Un servicio, tres formas de usarlo" / "One Service, Three Ways to Use It" described 1:1 coaching, All-Access and one-off plan sales as one service. They are three products at three prices with three service levels.* **Now "Una metodología, tres formas de acceder a ella"** *(Iván's own wording) — which is the true claim: the testing, the zones and the progression model are shared; the product is not.*

**The intro was defensive and has been replaced.** *"Sin niveles, sin letra chica. Testing obligatorio, feedback semanal todos los lunes, y ajustes cuando los necesitás." — Iván's read, and he is right: it argues against an accusation nobody made, and "sin niveles" actively contradicts the section it introduces, which is three tiers.* **Now: the method is the same in all three; what changes is how much coach you get and what it costs.**

🚨 ***The substantive finding: the homepage stated neither of the two axes the three offers actually differ on.*** *Iván named them — price and level of feedback — and the section carried no price at all and no ordering by service level, so it read as three unrelated products rather than as one ladder.* **Fixed structurally, not just in prose:** cards reordered **Coaching → All-Access → Plans** (descending on both axes, and it puts All-Access in the centre position, which was Iván's ask), and each card gained a `.card-meta` line — `$149/mes · seguimiento semanal`, `$39.99/mes · autoguiado, todo incluido`, `Desde $19.99 · pago único`. *Prices render from `site.json` and `allAccess.json`; the plan count from `plans.counts.total`. The card had read "+300 planes" against a real 328 — the same hand-typed-number defect as "Ocho herramientas", found while rewriting rather than by a check.*

**Two measure caps removed**, both reported by Iván as text "only using half the area": `.cred-band-lead` outright, and `.section-intro` via a new `.section-intro--full` modifier. *The 65ch cap is correct over prose and wrong over a full-width grid, where the heading and the grid both span the container and only the intro stops at ~57% — which reads as a layout fault rather than as a measure. The modifier keeps the default for the many intros that sit over body copy.*

⚠️ **Process note, worth more than any of the above.** *The first attempt at this edit sliced each homepage from the first `<span class="label">` in the file — which is in the **hero**, not the services section — and silently destroyed the `<h1>`, the hero copy and both hero CTAs on all three homepages. The build succeeded. The templates were valid. It was caught only because a browser check went looking for `#coaching` and the selector returned null.* **Restored from `git show HEAD:<path>` and redone anchored on `<section id="coaching">`, with assertions that the hero and the `<h1>` still exist after the edit.** ***A structural edit driven by a positional match needs an anchor unique to the thing being edited and a post-condition that names what must survive*** *— "the build passed" cannot distinguish a rewritten section from a deleted one.* **Note also that `git checkout --` cannot restore files over the device bridge (`unable to unlink: Operation not permitted`); `git show HEAD:<path> > file` is the working equivalent.**

### Same-day, fourth pass — the service cards became bulleted, and an offer change surfaced through a copy edit

**The three homepage service cards moved from one prose paragraph each to bullets** (`.card-list`), because the section exists to be *compared* and a reader comparing three options scans for differences rather than reading three paragraphs. One item per card is highlighted blue and bold (`.card-list-hl`) — the inclusion rather than a description. **Plan links gained the missing cycling hub, sentence-case SEO anchor text ("Planes de entrenamiento de …"), and EN/PT link lists that did not exist at all** (EN: running, cycling, swimming, ironman, weight-loss; PT: maratona, ciclismo, ironman — each language links only the hubs it actually has). **The plans card reverted to the per-language count** (`plans.counts.byLang.<lang>`) on Iván's instruction: a buyer of a single plan cares about plans they can read.

🚨 ***The important part of this pass was not a build. Writing one bullet surfaced a live rule that forbade it.***

*Iván asked for "TrainingPeaks Premium incluido" on the coaching card.* **`triaperformance-pricing-and-positioning.md` said, explicitly and with reasoning: "It must NOT be added to the coaching page or `sales-playbook.md` A5 — those describe the standard Private offer, which does not include Premium."** *Premium was the **referral reward** — $9/month bought for referred athletes specifically, so that a referred athlete got something a $149 athlete arriving any other way did not. Close #1 corroborated it from the other side: the TrainingPeaks invoice carries **1.4839 Premium licences**, i.e. Premium for about two people, not thirty-six.*

**Held the bullet and asked rather than shipping it.** *Iván's answer: the offer has changed — Premium is now included for everyone.* **So it shipped, on both coaching surfaces of all three homepages** *(the services card and the $149 price card — leaving it off one would have contradicted the other within a single scroll)*, **and the rule was struck at its owner with the decision, the date and the consequences recorded.**

⚠️ ***Three consequences, all logged in `open-loops.md`, none of them visible from the copy edit that caused them:*** *(1)* **the referral program's reward is gone** — the program now stands on the $50 gift card alone, and its disclosure message has not sent yet, so this is decidable before it becomes a promise; *(2)* **`sales-playbook.md` A5 still does not mention Premium**, so the website promises something the closing message a lead actually receives does not; *(3)* **the cost turned out to be $18/month, upper bound $27** — ~~*upper bound 36 × $9 = $324/month against August operating costs of $245.25, more than doubling the cost base*~~ 🚨 ***an estimate wrong by 12–18×, corrected by Iván and struck within the hour.*** *TrainingPeaks pays Premium for CoachMatch athletes as part of the 20% commission, and All-Access carries its own $9 deducted before payout, so only PRIVATE athletes cost anything: `data/monthly_close/2026-08-roster.csv` reads COACHMATCH 35 · PRIVATE 4 · ALL_ACCESS 3, with `premium_fee` = 9 on exactly three rows in the whole file.* **The owning doc had already written, in bold, "never derived here — counted at decision time from the live roster"; it was derived anyway, off the wrong headcount, on a decision being taken live.** ***A standing rule that says "count this, do not derive it" is worth reading before writing the number rather than after — and the roster that answers it exactly was one file away.***

***The transferable lesson is about the knowledge base rather than the site.*** *A one-line copy request ran into a documented prohibition that was three files away from the file being edited, and the prohibition turned out to be correct, load-bearing for a different initiative, and — as of that afternoon — obsolete.* **The rule earned its keep by being findable at the moment of the edit, not by being right forever.** *That is the argument for writing the reasoning next to the rule instead of only the rule: "do not add Premium to the coaching page" alone would have looked like a style preference and been silently overridden.*
